### 9.22 — `zeroEgressOnly` must mean what its contract says (⚠ rule-5 FALSE-ASSURANCE fix — ⛔ BRIEF PREMISE WAS INVERTED, RE-BRIEF REQUIRED)
- [x] DONE · `69b10883` · 2026-07-29 · ⚠ rule-5 (Step-9 routed to the lead) — shipped on **brief 215** (`@57d46c82`), not 203. **All three producers now derive from `isZeroEgressOnlyWorkspace`**, verified by the orchestrator in the COMMITTED file rather than the live tree (per the L83 amendment): `boot.ts:588` `isZeroEgressOnlyWorkspace(got.value)` · `egressRevoke.ts:92` `isZeroEgressOnlyWorkspace(revoked)` (derived from the just-written state — no second read, no re-opened TOCTOU) · **`boot.ts:543` `failClosedEgress` → `zeroEgressOnly: false`.** No fourth producer. +233-line `zeroEgressOnlyDerivation.test.ts`.
⭐ **THE FAIL-CLOSED DIRECTION INVERTED, and it was the slice's sharpest point — found only by reading the code while briefing, by neither the lead nor the owner's ruling.** `failClosedEgress` returned `zeroEgressOnly: true` and its comment called that the "safe default" — **which it WAS**, under `!acknowledged` ("we didn't confirm an ack, so assume the restrictive posture"). Under option C, `true` asserts **provably** local-only, and **a store fault establishes nothing** — so fault→`true` would have become a false assurance on the exact surface the owner checks after a revoke: *this arc's defect class, reintroduced by the function named to prevent it, with no code change at the guard site.* The implementer confirmed the inversion independently with a better argument than the brief's — `validWorkspace`'s own `capabilityDefaults: {}` already fails non-vacuity, so a fault claiming `true` is *less* justified than the baseline. ⚠ **General form worth carrying: a guard can be correct, then become a defect because the MEANING of what it guards changed underneath it.** Strongest evidence yet for the 24.6 pre-go-live audit.
⚠ **L69 confirmed live:** `egressCommands.test.ts:87` and `:182` asserted `zeroEgressOnly === true` after a revoke and went RED **for the right reason**; flipping them was disclosed as a test-semantics change on a safety pin, not folded into "updated tests." **7 further incidental `true` literals were counted and correctly left alone** (hand-rolled fakes over the tRPC projection layer, never routed through the real derivation) — the count reported per L64 rather than the conclusion. ⚠ The brief cited `:83`/`:178` from L69's own text, which had gone **stale**; the implementer corrected it by reading the file, and L69's citations are now fixed (an L71 instance inside L69). *(Did not ship 2026-07-26: brief 203's model of `allowedProcessors` was INVERTED and the slice as scoped would have created a NEW false assurance. Nothing committed, nothing staged.)*
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5 (EgressPolicy / egress veto), §16, REQ-S-002 · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** desktop #8 (the zero-egress rendering; its acceptance bullet stays UNMET with NO approved deferral)
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/boot.ts` (the `egressStatus` producer + `failClosedEgress`) · `apps/worker/src/composition/egressRevoke.ts` (a SECOND hardcoded producer) · `apps/worker/src/api/procedures/systemHealth.ts` (contract doc) · `packages/policy/src/processors.ts` (the predicate) · tests
**The original defect stands:** `boot.ts` computes `zeroEgressOnly` as `!employerRawEgressAcknowledged`, but the field's documented contract (`systemHealth.ts:36-46`) is "pinned to a LOCAL zero-egress provider". A **second** producer, `egressRevoke.ts:84`, returns `zeroEgressOnly: true` as a hardcoded literal immediately after a revoke — with a comment three lines above telling the next person to keep it in sync, which is precisely the arrangement that failed (a comment is not a mechanism).
**⛔ THE CORRECTED MODEL (verified in source by worker AND the orchestrator, 2026-07-26 — brief 203 got this backwards):**
- `packages/policy/src/egress.ts:138-144` — an EGRESSING route (`proc !== null`) with an **EMPTY** `allowedProcessors` ⇒ `PROCESSOR_NOT_ALLOWED` **deny**. **Empty allowlist = every egress route denied = the STRONGEST zero-egress state.** Brief 203's a4 specified `false` for it — wrong, and vacuous in practice since every real workspace lands there.
- `egress.ts:158` — a genuine loopback-local route (`proc === null`) hits "always egress-safe" and allows **without ever consulting the allowlist**. So a local provider NEVER needs to be in it — the contract states this verbatim (`egress-policy.ts:34-36`: "Local Ollama/LM Studio are non-egress and are never required processors"), and `processorOfRoute` returns `null` for them.
- **Therefore `["ollama"]` in an EGRESS allowlist can only mean a REMOTE ollama endpoint approved as a destination** (the tunneled-local hole `processors.ts:7-9` exists to close). Brief 203's a1 specified `true` for it — which would claim "local-only" for exactly the config meaning remote-ollama receives content, RAW INCLUDED. That is a NEW false assurance of the class this task exists to kill, and it launders `LOCAL_PROVIDERS` in the way that file's own comment forbids. Not reachable today (`provisionWorkspace.ts:64/72` seeds `[claude]` or `[]`) but `SOW_EGRESS_ALLOWED_PROCESSORS` is an operator-set comma-split at `boot.ts:1206` — one env var reaches the dangerous branch.
- **Root cause, recorded because it is instructive:** the orchestrator's Q1 ruling ("empty ⇒ false; unconfigured must never read as a guarantee") was sound reasoning applied to the WRONG STATE. For this field empty ≠ unconfigured; empty = deny-all. Following the brief faithfully WAS the failure mode (contracts L57 process half).
**⭐ RESOLVED — OWNER RULED OPTION C (2026-07-27, via lead), then AMENDED THE SAME DAY to THREE conjuncts.**
⭐ **FINAL SEMANTIC — `zeroEgressOnly` is true ONLY when ALL THREE hold:** (1) **the model config is NON-EMPTY** (a real configured setup — the third conjunct, added by owner ruling below); (2) the provider matrix resolves **local-only** (`allowedProviders ⊆ LOCAL_PROVIDERS` + `capabilityDefaults` routes loopback-local by `processorOfRoute === null` + `rawCloudEgressEnabled` strictly false); (3) **both allowlists are empty**. Fail-closed on every conjunct.
**⛔ THE THIRD CONJUNCT — owner ruling 2026-07-27, and WHY it exists (do not drop it as belt-and-braces):** provint's `security-reviewer` established that conjuncts (2) and (3) are **NOT INDEPENDENT at the default state** — `provisionWorkspace` seeds BOTH allowlists `[claude]`, so "both allowlists empty" **IS** the never-provisioned (or 9.29-wiped) state, and that same absence leaves `providerMatrix` empty, satisfying (2) **vacuously**. **One missing event zeroed both**, so an unconfigured workspace reported the strongest safety claim the system can make. **Option C was chosen precisely because it was said to require two independent failures — that property did not hold at exactly the state option A was rejected for.** The owner's fix: require a non-empty model config, so **an unconfigured workspace now reads FALSE — honest, because the app does not know yet and must not reassure.** ⚠ **PROVENANCE, recorded straight at the lead's instruction: the "two independent failures" framing was the LEAD's and was WRONG.** provint did not find a flaw in their own work — they found a flaw in the ruling's stated basis, while holding a green slice they could have shipped. *(Producer half `5571af93` — supersedes the dead `db423f35`, message-only amend, tree byte-identical — landed the TWO-conjunct form before the amendment; #21 reopened to fold in the third rather than ship-then-amend a knowingly-weaker semantic into history.)*
⭐ **PRODUCER HALF = TWO COMMITS, BOTH REQUIRED: `5571af93` (two-conjunct) + `2356e9b4` (adds the owner's third conjunct, supersedes it).** ⚠ **Citing only the first is citing a semantic the owner REJECTED.** They could not be squashed: by the time the ruling landed, `5571af93` was no longer HEAD (`473d1b62` sat on top) and the shared tree held four other implementers' uncommitted slices, so a rebase would have meant stashing work that was not provint's mid-slice. The weaker semantic remains reachable at `5571af93` but **nothing consumes it** — no producer is wired. A squash is offered once the tree is clean; until then **both hashes travel together**.
⛔ **⭐ #1 RESUME ITEM — READ BEFORE WIRING THE PREDICATE TO ANY CONSUMER. ⚠ SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-07-28 — the third conjunct LANDED at `2356e9b4`; what follows describes the interim state at `5571af93` and is retained because the ORDERING RULE below still governs.** The producer half first committed at **`5571af93`** with the **TWO-conjunct** predicate — i.e. **a semantic the owner explicitly REJECTED**. Verified in source by the lead and re-verified independently: `processors.ts:198` is exactly `isLocalOnlyProviderMatrix(...) && hasNoApprovedEgressDestination(...)`, and `everyProviderIsLocal([])` **falls through to `true`** because the dense scan's loop body never executes on an empty array — so an unconfigured workspace (empty `allowedProviders`, empty `capabilityDefaults`, empty allowlists) evaluates **`true`**, exactly the vacuity the third conjunct exists to close. **Cause was a lost race, not judgement:** the owner ruled ~02:26 and provint committed ~02:32; the ruling did not reach them in time. Nobody ignored anything.
**EFFECT TODAY: NONE — and this is the only reason it is safe to leave.** Nothing consumes the predicate: worker's 9.22 is unstarted and desktop #8 is unbuilt, so no live surface reports anything. ⛔ **THE ORDERING IS THE SAFETY PROPERTY: whoever resumes MUST add the third conjunct BEFORE wiring the predicate to any consumer, never after.** A consumer landing first makes the rejected semantic *live*. **Status at pause:** the conjunct was dispatched into #21 as a continuation and reached RED-with-tests-rewritten before the owner's idle instruction landed; whether it committed depends on the A/B call recorded in the team handoff. **Verify `processors.ts` in source before assuming either way** — do not trust this line, or any message, over the file.

The ruling stands and the semantic is right; what is missing is a writer. *(Verified independently: the only non-empty `allowedProviders` in production is `boot.ts:1194`, which is a `ResolvedWorkspacePolicy` — in-memory broker/proof-spine config that never reaches `workspaceConfig.upsert`. Worker independently identified that same false positive during 9.29's trace — two implementers, different slices, same conclusion.)*
⛔ **TWO ROUTING CONSEQUENCES:** **(a) desktop #8 must render `false` as UNKNOWN, never as "cloud egress possible"** — that inversion is the same L56 defect facing the other way, and it is now the *likely* reading since `false` is the only value the field can take. **(b) Populating `providerMatrix` is ADJACENT TO BUT NOT THE SAME WORK as task 9.29** — 9.29 stops the wipe; **nothing yet does the write**. Tracked as **task 9.32**.
⛔ **SCOPE BOUND (provint, same slice) — the name over-promises and this is load-bearing for desktop #8's COPY:** these predicates cover **MODEL-PROVIDER egress only**. Connectors and Tool-Gateway writes never consult `egressPolicy.allowedProcessors`, so an armed-connector workspace can satisfy every conjunct and still egress. Any surface promising "nothing leaves this machine" re-creates the L56 class **in words**, with a wider blast radius than in code because copy reads as authoritative. **The owner's reasoning, recorded because the next reader needs the why, not just the what:** this field's entire job is to reassure, so it should be hard to make it lie. A and B each have a specific case where they claim safety wrongly — **A** cannot distinguish a deliberately-locked-down workspace from one never provisioned; **B** ignores a leftover cloud destination sitting alongside a local matrix. **C requires two independent things to go wrong before the app lies to the owner.** Six instances of wrongly-asserted safety in one round is the evidence base for choosing the conservative shape. ⭐ **SCOPE WIDENING INTO `providerMatrix` IS EXPLICITLY AUTHORIZED** — the corrected brief must SAY SO plainly; the ABSENCE of that authorization is precisely what made the original brief 203 wrong. ⚠ **Coupled durability precondition — task 9.29:** re-provision currently RESETS `providerMatrix`, so under C the derivation's input is volatile until 9.29 lands. *(Options as originally framed, retained for audit:)* **(A)** both allowlists EMPTY ⇒ true. ### 9.23 — Re-provision must NOT silently restore a revoked egress ack (⚠ rule-5 FAIL-OPEN fix)
- [x] DONE · `144ae6f1` · 2026-07-26 · ⚠ rule-5 · **9.21's owner-ordered hard-edge prerequisite is SATISFIED.**
⚠ **The MECHANISM that shipped here was later SUPERSEDED by 9.30 `615394a8` — deliberately, not dropped.** The `existing.value.egressPolicy` carry-forward and its `WorkspaceSchema.parse` re-gate were DELETED, because `updateProvisioningFields` narrows the same-type write to name/vaultRoot/brainId and therefore never touches egress state at all — so no stored blob re-crosses into a write and the re-gate's premise evaporates rather than being ignored. **The Done-when still holds and is pinned at HEAD:** `re_provision_preserves_a_revoked_ack` · `fresh_create_still_seeds` · `different_type_still_rejected` — all green in `apps/worker/test/composition/provision-preserves-egress-posture.test.ts` (21/21, orchestrator-verified at commit `2b84eb0f`, not the live tree). *(State line reconciled 2026-07-29: the checkbox read `[ ] OPEN — prerequisite for 9.21` while this phase's own acceptance block already credited `144ae6f1` as landed. An orchestrator reading task sections instead of prose routes around 9.21 as blocked.)*
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5 (rule-5 egress veto), §16 (audit) · **Depends:** 9.10-B revoke (`225c10ca`), 9.10 flip (`bcde3d61`) · **Blocks:** 9.10 completion · 9.21 (idempotent scaffold repair/resume — a re-provision path)
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/composition/provisionWorkspace.ts` (`seedCloudCopilotAllowlist` call site + the existence-check branches) · worker tests
`provisionWorkspace` seeds the egress allowlist BEFORE the existence check, and for `employer_work` that seed sets `employerRawEgressAcknowledged:true` + a fresh `acknowledgedAt`. The existence check's same-type branch deliberately falls through ("an idempotent overwrite") to `workspaceConfig.upsert(workspace)` — which writes the freshly-seeded policy over the stored one. **Consequence: the 9.10-B owner revoke is not durable.** After a revoke, any later `onboarding.createWorkspace` for the same workspace silently restores ack=true with NO audit row and NO owner confirm — so the revoke's safety guarantee currently holds only as long as nobody re-provisions. Not casually reachable today (the onboarding mount is gated post-onboarding), but **9.21 is exactly a re-provision path**, so this goes live when 9.21 lands. **This is not a missing rule — it is worker L30 UNDER-APPLIED.** L30's own wording makes a workspace-bound record's binding anchor immutable-by-default and names *"a field deriving the isolation classification / **egress-veto applicability**"* as such an anchor — and `employerRawEgressAcknowledged` IS the egress-veto applicability signal. The get-before-upsert guard L30 mandates was implemented for `type` and never extended to the `egressPolicy` fields the SAME `upsert` overwrites; the `get` already happens for the type check, its result simply isn't used to preserve egress state. Fix: seed ONLY on the `not_found` fresh-create branch; on a same-type overwrite carry `existing.value.egressPolicy` forward VERBATIM (a re-provision changes name/vaultRoot, never egress posture). This is a fail-open closure, not an egress opening, so it needs no owner crossing — but it IS a rule-5 commit (Step-9 → lead) and wants `security-reviewer=invariant`. **Done-when:** a revoke followed by a same-type re-provision leaves ack=false (`re_provision_preserves_a_revoked_ack`), a fresh create still seeds as before, and the type-immutability guard is unchanged. *(origin: 2026-07-26 9.10-C Step-9 Finding 1, desktop; verified in code by the orchestrator)*

### 9.24 — The Copilot egress notice's ABSENCE must be derived (⚠ rule-5 fail-open-by-omission)
- [x] DONE · `d0886ea4` · 2026-07-26 · ⚠ **NOT in the stale-checkbox set the lead flagged — found by this round's WIDER audit of every Phase-9 checkbox against history.**
**Closed via the branch its own text authorized — *"unreachable ⇒ a documented invariant + a pin that keeps it so."*** A **characterization slice with ZERO production change**: traced first, implemented second. **Case 3 has no live route** — `egressProcessor` is `.optional()` rather than `.catch()`, so it can never be silently stripped (only the whole answer can fail), and a failed ask renders an explicit `ASK_FAILED` turn rather than a plausible answer with a missing notice. Both synthesis adapters additionally fail closed on a non-Claude route before any egress, and `buildCopilotDeps` pairs synthesis+routeSelector under one ternary so they cannot skew.

**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5 (egress veto / consent posture), §10 (UI-safe read-model), §11, REQ-S-002 · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** —
**Files:** `apps/desktop/renderer/surfaces/copilot/Copilot.tsx:96-104` · the `CopilotTurnView` mapper in `renderer/lib/` · desktop tests
`Copilot.tsx` renders its cloud-egress consent notice iff `turn.egressProcessor !== undefined`, so the notice's ABSENCE reads to the user as "nothing was disclosed because there was nothing to disclose". Per the contract (`packages/contracts/src/api/ui-safe.ts:474-484`) absence is **overloaded three ways**: (1) a local/zero-egress answer — correct; (2) **non-Employer-Work cloud egress** — content DID go to a cloud model and the owner deliberately chose no notice for that case, so this is an OWNER-CHOSEN POSTURE and ⛔ **out of scope** (changing it is an escalation, not a fix); (3) **the signal did not arrive** — producer didn't set it, a projection dropped it, validation rejected an over-length/multi-line label, or an older payload lacks the field. **Case 3 is the defect**: "we had no data" and "nothing to warn about" render identically. Same defect class as the hardcoded chrome pill (contracts L56) but the opposite construction — a claim IMPLIED by a missing element rather than asserted from a constant. **First deliverable is a REACHABILITY TRACE** (can the field be absent on a turn that egressed employer content? with `file:line` evidence), because the honest scope depends on the answer: unreachable ⇒ a documented invariant + a pin that keeps it so; reachable via a drop/validation path ⇒ a renderer fix; reachable because the producer may not set it ⇒ producer-first (worker leg first). A renderer that genuinely cannot distinguish safe-absent from dropped-absent means the fix is a distinct tri-state on the contract — a frozen-contract + producer change, orchestrator-routed, NOT a desktop-local edit. **Done-when:** the unknown state never renders as the safe/silent one, case 1 stays silent, case 2 is unchanged AND pinned so it can't drift under cover of a fix, and the unknown rendering discloses nothing raw. **Recorded owner tension (NOT a defect, for deliberate revisit):** case 2 means a personal-workspace answer synthesized in the CLOUD renders identically to a local one — the UI cannot distinguish them, by the owner's own "notice applies to Employer-Work" choice. Left unchanged and PINNED unchanged so it cannot drift under cover of a fix; changing it is an owner posture decision, not engineering. *(origin: 2026-07-26 #10 Step-9 — desktop's correction to L56's scope; pre-existing, not from this round's diffs)*

### 9.29 — Re-provision silently resets `providerMatrix` + `defaultVisibility` (the fail-CLOSED half of 9.23)
- [x] DONE · `a0eafd22` (reviewed decision + writer census) → made STRUCTURAL by `615394a8` · 2026-07-26 / 07-28 · **9.21's gate is SATISFIED.**
⭐ **Closed via the Done-when's SECOND branch — *"or an explicit, reviewed decision records why a reset is correct for each"* — NOT the carry-forward branch. This distinction is the whole slice, so do not read the tick as "the fields are now carried forward."** The trace the slice was briefed to produce found **no post-provision writer** of `providerMatrix` or `defaultVisibility`: only `provisionWorkspace` (which rebuilds them from `defaultWorkspace`) and `egressRevoke` (which touches `egressPolicy` alone) write the config store. So the stored values can only ever BE the defaults, and the re-provision "reset" overwrites identical values — preserving state nothing can yet change is speculative work that READS as safety, and for `providerMatrix` it would have carried a possibly-permissive `rawCloudEgressEnabled` forward for no realizable benefit (the same argument that kept this out of 9.23). What shipped instead is the **writer-census tripwire** that fails at the moment the question becomes real, across three surfaces (repo-type writers · direct drizzle writers · SQL migrations), anchored to `git ls-files` so build artefacts cannot produce a false red. **Non-vacuity proven by experiment, not argument:** a real third writer was added under `composition/`, the census FIRED, and it was removed. `615394a8` then upgraded the invariant from **documentary to STRUCTURAL** by making provisioning stop writing those fields at all — exactly the second-order prize task 9.30 predicted. **Accepted residual, recorded in-code:** an out-of-band change (a sqlite CLI edit, a restore from an older snapshot) can harden one of these fields with no code writer to catch it; nothing goes red and the next re-provision silently reverts it. *(State line reconciled 2026-07-29 — the checkbox read `[ ] OPEN — ⛔ Blocks 9.21`.)*
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5 (egress/provider posture), §11 (onboarding / re-provision semantics) · **Depends:** 9.23 (same call site, same branch 2b) · **Blocks:** 9.21 · ⛔ **9.22 (lands BEFORE or WITH it — see below)** · **Brief:** `docs/briefs/209-9.29-reprovision-preserves-provider-posture.md` (`@019090da`)
_(No contract-model anchor is cited: the slice changes which BRANCH writes which fields, not the aggregate's shape — `Model field changes: none`. An earlier draft cited the Workspace-aggregate section and `spec-lint` correctly rejected it as outside Phase 9's anchor set.)_
**⛔ WHY THIS BLOCKS 9.22 (lead ruling 2026-07-27; a fresh implementer reading only 9.22 would NOT derive this):** 9.22 is what **renders** the zero-egress claim to the owner, and under option C that claim derives from `providerMatrix`. If a re-provision can silently wipe that input, shipping 9.22 first opens a **live window** in which the surface reports zero-egress *vacuously* — true by accident, for a reason the owner never chose, **on the exact screen they check to confirm a revoke landed.** That is the round's sixth-instance shape with extra steps. Order: 9.29 before or with 9.22.
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/composition/provisionWorkspace.ts` (the same-type overwrite branch) · worker tests
`provisionWorkspace.ts:146` calls `defaultWorkspace()` **unconditionally**, rebuilding the WHOLE aggregate from spec on every provision — so a same-type overwrite resets not only the egress ack (task 9.23) but also **`providerMatrix`** → `{allowedProviders: [], capabilityDefaults: {}, rawCloudEgressEnabled: false}` and **`defaultVisibility`** → `"isolated"` (`packages/contracts/src/models/workspace.ts:110,129-134`). Same class as 9.23 — provisioning rewriting durable state it did not create — but the **safety direction is opposite**: these are fail-CLOSED clobbers (configuration/availability loss; `rawCloudEgressEnabled` resets to the RESTRICTIVE value), where the egress ack was the fail-OPEN one. `dataOwner` cannot drift — it derives from `type`, which the existing immutability guard already pins.
**⚠ Why this is NOT a trivial extension of 9.23 and was deliberately held out of it:** carrying `egressPolicy` forward preserves a **revoked** (restrictive) state; carrying `providerMatrix` forward would preserve a possibly-**permissive** one (`rawCloudEgressEnabled: true`). Same code shape, different safety argument — it needs its own review posture rather than riding in on a rule-5 slice's coattails.
**⚠ Interaction with task 9.22, for whoever holds the A/B/C ruling:** options **B and C derive `zeroEgressOnly` FROM `providerMatrix`**. Under those options this reset silently changes the derivation's INPUT — a wiped matrix satisfies all three of B's conditions vacuously (`[] ⊆ LOCAL_PROVIDERS`, no `capabilityDefaults` routes, `rawCloudEgressEnabled: false`) ⇒ the derived claim reads `true`. **Stated precisely (not overstated): the resulting claim is not FALSE** — an empty matrix genuinely denies, the same reasoning that makes 9.22's empty allowlist the strongest zero-egress state — **but the owner's configured posture has been destroyed and the surface would report zero-egress for a reason the owner never chose.** A derivation is only as honest as the durability of its input. Worth weighing when picking B vs C; it does not by itself argue against either.
**Done-when:** a same-type re-provision carries `providerMatrix` + `defaultVisibility` forward from the stored aggregate (or an explicit, reviewed decision records why a reset is correct for each), a fresh create still applies the safe defaults, and the name/vaultRoot update still takes effect. *(origin: 2026-07-27 9.23 Step-2.5 Q5, worker; independently verified in source by the orchestrator)*

### 9.30 — `provisionWorkspace`'s `get`→`upsert` TOCTOU window (the residual race 9.23 left)
- [x] DONE · `615394a8` · 2026-07-28 · **9.21's gate is SATISFIED.**
**Option A as approved:** `updateProvisioningFields` narrows the same-type branch to `name`/`vaultRoot`/`brainId` and `insertIfAbsent` makes the create branch insert-only, so provisioning **never writes the egress column at all** — a concurrent revoke has nothing to lose and **the race cannot occur rather than being caught.** Detecting the conflict was the obvious fix and the weaker one.
⭐ **BOTH BRANCHES MATTERED, and an earlier version of this change claimed the race was closed while leaving it open:** a provision that read `not_found` and then lost the window to both a create and a revoke blind-updated its freshly-seeded `ack=true` back over the owner's decision. Found in review, reproduced with a failing test, then fixed. **Narrowing one branch while another keeps the blind whole-row write moves the defect rather than removing it.**
**Pinned at HEAD:** `interleaved_revoke_is_not_silently_reverted` · `create_branch_conflict_does_not_restore_a_revoked_ack` · `provisioning_write_cannot_touch_egress_state` · `same_type_write_updates_only_provisioning_owned_fields`. Both new mutators carry dual-dialect contract coverage.
⚠ **RESIDUAL → task #38, and it is a follow-on on a DIFFERENT write path, NOT an unfinished part of 9.30:** the *revoke* side still does a whole-aggregate upsert, so a concurrent RENAME can be lost — **benign direction** (the opposite of the fail-open this task closed). Do not re-open 9.30 for it. *(State line reconciled 2026-07-29 — the checkbox read `[ ] OPEN` while the acceptance block already credited it.)*
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5 (rule-5 egress veto), §4 (operational store), §16 · **Depends:** 9.23 · ⛔ **Blocks: 9.21 (lands BEFORE or WITH it)**
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/composition/provisionWorkspace.ts` · `packages/db` adapters · worker tests
9.23 closed the routine fail-open, but the existence-check `get` → `upsert` remains a **non-atomic read-modify-write** over an unconditional `onConflictDoUpdate` in both adapters. **A `revokeEgressAck` landing BETWEEN the `get` and the `upsert` is silently reverted** — 9.23's own defect narrowed to a race, and still with no audit row. Low likelihood on a single-user local deployment, but it is the honest answer to "is any window left?". ⚠ Asymmetry worth noting: the sibling `workspaceRegistry.ts:40-46` carries an explicit `arch_gap (concurrency)` note for exactly this class; this path carried none. ⛔ **SEQUENCING — 9.30 lands BEFORE or WITH 9.21, and "low likelihood" was an ORCHESTRATOR framing that did not survive verification (worker, 2026-07-28).** The race needs a re-provision concurrent with a revoke, which today needs a user doing two things at once. **9.21 (repair/resume) makes it a BACKGROUND re-provision** — the identical argument that made 9.23 must-land-before-9.21. It is structurally reachable already: both are tRPC procedures and Node interleaves at every `await`.
⛔ **THE SIBLING'S `arch_gap` DOES NOT TRANSFER — do not "match the house answer" (worker, 2026-07-28; this overturned the orchestrator's own default vote).** `workspaceRegistry.ts:39-47` accepts its race, but its stated justification is *"the direction is fail-SAFE — a dropped id becomes INVISIBLE (its scoped reads fail closed), never a cross-workspace leak — and a re-provision repairs it."* **Both halves INVERT here:** a lost update reverts a revoke (ack false→**true**) ⇒ fail-**OPEN**, and **a re-provision is what CAUSES it**, not what repairs it. Copying the conclusion while discarding the premise that earned it is L56/L72 in a new costume. So a documented-`arch_gap` option cannot borrow that justification; it would need its own, and the only honest one is "fail-open, reachable once 9.21 lands, accepted anyway."
**Fix direction — OPTION A, approved 2026-07-28: a PARTIAL update on the same-type branch.** Create = full insert; same-type = update ONLY the fields provisioning owns (`name`, `markdownRepoPath`, `gbrainBrainId`). Provisioning then **never writes the egress column at all**, so a concurrent revoke cannot be clobbered — **the race is eliminated by construction rather than detected.** Second-order prize: it makes **9.29's invariant STRUCTURAL rather than documentary** (provisioning stops writing state it does not own) and retires 9.23's carry-forward as unnecessary. Conditions: keep the type-immutability guard; remove 9.23's now-dead carry **deliberately, with a pin** proving the new write path cannot touch egress state; **state in-code why the `.strict()` re-parse is no longer needed** (under A the stored blob never re-crosses into a write, so L76's concern evaporates rather than being ignored); the repo-interface growth's fake ripple lands as its own L50 reconciliation commit. **Done-when:** a revoke interleaved between the existence check and the write either wins or fails loudly — never silently reverts. *(origin: 2026-07-27 9.23 Step-9 flag 1, worker)*

### 9.31 — The egress revoke is durable per workspace ROW, not per VAULT (⚠ bounds the 9.23 claim)
- [x] DONE · `615394a8` · 2026-07-28 · (never a `/phase-exit 9` gate — `Blocks: —`; it BOUNDS the claim 9.21 leans on)
**Closed via the Done-when's SECOND branch — *"or the limitation is recorded where a reader asking about revoke durability will meet it."*** `markdownRepoPath` has **zero production consumers** (the runtime vault comes from `config.vaultRoot`), so "two workspaces sharing a vault root" is **not a coherent runtime state today** — the worker has exactly ONE vault, from config. The field is write-only: persisted at provisioning, never read. So the deliverable was documentation **plus a tripwire** — `markdownRepoPath_has_no_production_consumer` fires the day anything reads it. The stale `backends.ts` claim that a deployment passes the workspace's `markdownRepoPath` is **corrected** (it passes `config.vaultRoot`).
⚠ **THE LOAD-BEARING PART IS DOCUMENTARY AND OUTLIVES THE FIX: *"is the revoke durable?" must never be answered unqualified.*** Durable per workspace **ROW**, not per **VAULT**. An unbounded yes is a safety claim asserted past the state that governs it — contracts **L56**, applied to this arc's own fix. **Whoever briefs 9.21 restates this bound rather than inheriting it.** *(State line reconciled 2026-07-29 — the checkbox read `[ ] OPEN`.)*
**Kind:** fix (hardening) · **Spec:** §5 (rule-5), §11 (onboarding), §6 (vault binding) · **Depends:** 9.23 · **Blocks:** —
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/api/procedures/onboarding.ts` · `apps/worker/src/composition/provisionWorkspace.ts` · worker tests
`createWorkspace` takes a **caller-chosen `id`** and nothing enforces uniqueness on `markdownRepoPath`, so a **NEW** `employer_work` workspace pointed at the **same vault root** is a fresh create — and therefore freshly seeded `ack=true`. 9.23 makes the revoke durable for the **ROW**; it does not make it durable for the **VAULT**. ⭐ **CONFIRMED NOT LIVE 2026-07-28, for a STRONGER reason than review assumed (worker):** `Workspace.markdownRepoPath` has **ZERO production consumers** — the runtime vault comes from boot config, not the workspace row (`backends.ts:804` → `createFsVault(config.vaultRoot ?? makeTmpVaultRoot())`). The field is **write-only**: persisted at provisioning, never read. So "two workspaces sharing a vault root" is **not a coherent runtime state today** — the worker has exactly ONE vault, from config. The deliverable is documentation. ⚠ **Side finding folded into this task:** `backends.ts:342` claims *"a deployment passes the workspace's `markdownRepoPath`"* — **false**, it passes `config.vaultRoot`; a stale doc claim about which state governs behaviour, same family as this round's others. ⚠ **The load-bearing part is documentary and outlives the fix:** *"is the revoke durable?" must never be answered unqualified.* An unbounded yes is a safety claim asserted past the state that governs it — contracts **L56**, the round's own thesis, applied to this round's own fix. Fix direction: uniqueness / conflict detection on `markdownRepoPath` at create, or an explicit recorded decision that vault-sharing is unsupported. **Done-when:** either a second workspace cannot silently bind an already-provisioned vault root, or the limitation is recorded where a reader asking about revoke durability will meet it. *(origin: 2026-07-27 9.23 Step-9 flag 2 — volunteered by the implementer, bounding their own fix)*

### 9.32 — Nothing ever writes a non-empty `providerMatrix` — `zeroEgressOnly` is unsatisfiable until something does
- [ ] DEFERRED → a later round · ⭐ **OWNER-APPROVED 2026-07-28 as a scoped ARC, not a slice** · ⭐⭐ **OWNER RULED 2026-07-29 ON THE SHAPE QUESTION — read the ruling before touching this**
⭐⭐ **OWNER RULING 2026-07-29 (via lead): AN EMPTY `providerMatrix` IS A *CORRECT STATE*, NOT A MISSING WRITER.** So:
- **`zeroEgressOnly === false` means NOT ESTABLISHED** — never "cloud egress is possible." The app **does not know**, so it must **neither reassure nor alarm**; both are claims it cannot support. Rendering `false` as "cloud egress is possible" is L56 inverted just as surely as rendering it "local-only" is.
- **Desktop #8 is UNBLOCKED and ships on this ruling**, with the surface rendering **UNKNOWN** and **no positive assurance available**. ⛔ **That absence IS the deliverable, not a shortfall** — say so in-code and here, or the next reader "fixes" it by seeding a default.
- ⛔ **The do-not-seed-a-default warning gets STRONGER, not weaker:** a writer defaulting to local-only would manufacture exactly the reassurance this entire arc exists to stop manufacturing.
- ⛔ **WHAT THE OWNER DID *NOT* DO: settle the product question.** They chose the **honest interim**. All three candidate shapes below (settings surface · detection probe · `ProvisioningProfile` extension) remain **open, none ruled out**. Do not read "empty is correct" as "no writer is ever needed" — read it as "an empty matrix is a truthful state to ship *while* the question is unanswered."
- ⛔⛔ **CRITICAL SEQUENCING TRAP — THE RULING IS ONE SLICE EARLY, AND TAKING IT LITERALLY SHIPS A RULE-5 FALSE ASSURANCE (found 2026-07-29, orchestrator-3, verified in source before authoring #8's brief; escalated).** **TWO DIFFERENT PREDICATES SHARE THE NAME `zeroEgressOnly`:**
  - **(1) What the ruling describes** — the option-C predicate (matrix all-local AND both allowlists empty AND matrix NON-EMPTY). Under it, an empty matrix ⇒ `false` ⇒ NOT ESTABLISHED. Coherent, and exactly what the owner ruled. ⛔ **It lives in `packages/policy` (`isZeroEgressOnlyWorkspace`, `5571af93`/`2356e9b4`, provint-reviewed clean) and has NO CONSUMER — task 9.22 is the consumer and 9.22 never shipped.**
  - **(2) What the desktop surface ACTUALLY reads** — `zeroEgressOnly: !acknowledged`, at `boot.ts:571` (also `boot.ts:534`, `egressRevoke.ts:84`). ⚠ So **`true` IS reachable in production today**, on **every personal workspace** (ack is always false there) — and those carry allowlist `[claude]`, i.e. cloud. `true` therefore asserts local-only for content that **does** egress.
  ⇒ **"`false` is the only reachable value" is TRUE OF (1) AND FALSE OF (2).** Rendering the live field would re-create the exact defect `#10` deleted from the chrome pill, on the one surface built to inform disclosure decisions. ⚠ **Desktop already refused this once and was right** — `apps/desktop/renderer/surfaces/workspace-settings/egress.tsx:15-21` documents the withholding verbatim: *"every personal workspace … would render a 'zero-egress only' assurance while its content does egress."* #8 as scoped asks them to undo a correct refusal.
  ⭐ **THE RULING'S REAL EFFECT: it unblocks 9.22, not #8.** 9.22 option C was held because its semantics were an unmade rule-5 decision; the owner has now made it, and "empty matrix is CORRECT" **is** option C's non-empty-matrix conjunct expressed as a product decision. It also ratifies the L77 concern (an unprovisioned workspace reporting the strongest available claim) as a DELIBERATE state rather than an accident.
  ⛔ **MANDATORY ORDER: 9.22 THEN #8.** (i) worker points `zeroEgressOnly` at the already-built, already-reviewed policy predicates (⚠ rule-5, own commit, Step-9 → lead); (ii) desktop renders UNKNOWN over a field that now honestly means it. **Delivering #8 first inverts the arc's whole purpose.**
- ⚠ **AND IT DOES NOT CLEAR PHASE 9 BY ITSELF.** #8 landing unblocks the 9.22/#8 chain; **9.21 and the remaining `[~]` legs still gate `/phase-exit 9`**, and the acceptance line was reconciled last round for a reason. Do not run the gate on #8's strength alone.
⛔ **THE ARC SHIPS WITH `zeroEgressOnly` HONESTLY READING UNKNOWN — that is the DELIVERABLE, not a shortfall.** Desktop #8 renders `false` as UNKNOWN / not-established. **Do NOT "fix" this by seeding a default:** a writer defaulting to local-only would manufacture exactly the reassurance this whole arc exists to stop manufacturing (contracts L56). The honest interim is the correct interim.
⛔ **THE PRODUCT QUESTION THE ARC MUST ANSWER FIRST — it is why this is an arc and not a slice:** *which providers does a workspace get, and chosen how?* Three candidate shapes, none obviously right: **(a)** extend `ProvisioningProfile` (but `presetProfiles.ts` — task 14.5, DONE — carries **no** `allowedProviders`/`capabilityDefaults` at all and types its two policy fields as *literal* `false`/`"isolated"` under `Object.freeze`, so a preset **structurally cannot arm anything**; extending it re-opens that deliberate design); **(b)** a settings surface (UI); **(c)** a local-model detection probe. **All three reach onboarding/settings UI or a detection step** — that is the size, not the write itself, which is trivial.
⭐ **ASSIGN IT TO WORKER, and tell them WHY — a successor reading a red census will otherwise think they broke it.** Making `providerMatrix` writable **creates the FIRST post-provision writer**, which **fires 9.29's writer census BY DESIGN** and re-opens the per-field carry-forward decision 9.29 deliberately deferred. The person who built that tripwire should be the one who trips it, using the **corrected consumer-direction table** (`providerMatrix` fail-closed at every consumer · `dataOwner` fail-OPEN at the **approval gate**, `approval-policy.ts:176` · `defaultVisibility` direction-dependent, permissive at that same gate) — **not** the wrong-mechanism version that briefly claimed the §5 veto branches on `dataOwner`.
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §5 (provider routing / egress posture), §11 (onboarding), §7 (provider matrix) · **Depends:** 9.29 (stops the wipe; this does the write) · **Blocks:** any surface that renders `zeroEgressOnly` as a positive claim (desktop #8)
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/composition/provisionWorkspace.ts` and/or a settings/config surface · worker tests
`defaultWorkspace()` seeds an **empty** `providerMatrix` and **no production path writes a non-empty one** — only `provisionWorkspace` (rebuilding from that default) and `egressRevoke` (`egressPolicy` only) write the aggregate. So the stored matrix is empty for **every** workspace, and 9.22's `zeroEgressOnly` — which requires a non-empty, all-local matrix — **can never be `true` in production**. ⚠ **Distinct from 9.29:** 9.29 stops re-provision from WIPING the matrix; this task is the missing WRITER. Fixing 9.29 alone preserves an empty matrix perfectly.
⚠ **Design question this must answer, not assume:** *should* provisioning populate a matrix at all, or is an empty matrix the correct default with population belonging to a later config surface? An empty matrix is fail-CLOSED (routes deny), so the current state is safe — **this is a missing capability, not a live defect**, and it must not be "fixed" by seeding a permissive default. Whoever takes it decides deliberately and records the reasoning.
**Done-when:** either a production path populates `providerMatrix` (with the seeded value's safety argued explicitly), or a recorded decision states that an empty matrix is correct and `zeroEgressOnly`'s positive branch is intentionally unreachable until a config surface exists — in which case desktop #8's copy must reflect that. *(origin: 2026-07-28 provint task #21 Finding; independently verified — `boot.ts:1194`'s non-empty `allowedProviders` is a `ResolvedWorkspacePolicy`, never persisted)*

### 9.33 — `processorOfRoute` is not total: a blank identity THROWS instead of denying (⚠ rule-5 fail-open-by-crash)
- [x] DONE · `ceb42987` · 2026-07-28 (⚠ state line reconciled 2026-07-29 — it still read OPEN while this phase's own acceptance block already listed `ceb42987` as landed; banked as the house rule the fix itself established: **a safety gate that THROWS where it should DENY hands its caller a crash instead of a denial**, now cited by the #45 constraint and by the #36 no-fix verdict)
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §5 (egress veto / processor identity), §16 (typed errors, never throw across a boundary) · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** — · **Brief:** `docs/briefs/210-9.33-processor-of-route-totality.md`
**Files:** `packages/policy/src/processors.ts` · `packages/policy/test/`
`processorOfRoute` documents itself pure and fail-closed, and its malformed-route branches correctly return `processorId("MALFORMED_ROUTE")` (egress, never non-egress). **Then it brands the route's raw identity, and the brand constructor THROWS on a blank string** — so `{provider: ""}` arriving from a deserialized row throws instead of denying. The guards are exhaustive over the cases their author enumerated; **the throw lives after them.** ⚠ **Why it is rule-5 and not tidiness:** this is the identity layer directly beneath the egress veto, so a throw on an untrusted route is a **fail-open-by-crash** — the caller receives an exception rather than a deny, and whether that denies depends entirely on the caller's error handling. provint contained it at their own boundary (one `try` ⇒ deny) during task #21, which protects *that* caller and leaves **every other one exposed** — hence a task rather than a call-site fix. **Done-when:** a blank/malformed identity on either branch denies rather than throws, **no well-formed route's egress classification moves** (the no-drift pin), and the caller audit is reported. *(origin: 2026-07-28 provint task #21 flag 3; contracts L78 is this task's generalization)*

### 9.34 — Brand the Copilot reply so `reply:` is uninhabitable by a hand-built literal
- [x] DONE · `d7a9b170` · 2026-07-29 (closes 9.28's relocated residual)
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §11 (Copilot surface), §5 (egress disclosure — ⚠ rule-5-adjacent) · **Depends:** 9.26, 9.28 · **Blocks:** —
**Files:** `apps/desktop/renderer/surfaces/copilot/Copilot.tsx` · `apps/desktop/test-dom/copilot-panel.test.tsx`
`CopilotTurnView.reply` and `CopilotAnswerView`'s prop were typed `UiSafeCopilotAnswer` — **structurally** satisfiable, so a hand-built literal omitting the `.optional()` `egressProcessor` rule-5 disclosure field still compiled and silently dropped the disclosure (9.28's honest bound). Closed with a **nominal brand**: `AdmittedCopilotAnswer`, mintable only by the exported `admitReply()` (internal cast after `.safeParse`), required at both chokepoints. ⭐ **The part worth keeping:** a SEPARATE unbranded `CopilotTurnSeed` was introduced for the mount-time seed prop, because that boundary genuinely receives candidate data — so the brand asserts something TRUE rather than being cast into place at the door. Both reviewers clean (security-reviewer at invariant scope, code-quality every-slice), each having independently mutation-verified the two `@ts-expect-error` pins by aliasing the brand back to the bare contract type and confirming the directives go unused. RED was earned: both pins compiled without error pre-brand ⇒ unused directive ⇒ typecheck RED. No `packages/contracts` change — the brand is a renderer-local nominal type over the existing contract, not a contract edit. *(origin: 2026-07-28 9.28 Step-9 residual, desktop)*

### 9.35 — No ErrorBoundary anywhere in `apps/desktop` — a render throw unmounts the whole root
- [x] DONE · `3f33c97b` · 2026-07-29 · **BOTH boundary sites shipped** (`main.tsx` around `<App/>`; `AppShell.tsx` around `{children}`) — neither alone closes the finding, since `App()` early-returns to `<Onboarding>` at `:214` and a per-surface boundary structurally cannot reach the first-run white-screen class. ⭐ **Rule 7 enforced STRUCTURALLY: `fallback` is typed `(reset) => ReactNode` with NO error parameter**, so a caller cannot render `error.message` or a stack — it is never handed one. Reset is route-keyed AND manual. ⚠ **Catches RENDER throws only** — async/handler failures stay with the house fold-to-`{ok:false}`, pinned as characterization so nobody later assumes the boundary superseded them; Copilot's `admitReply` deliberately untouched. ⚠ **Site B's wiring is test-verified; Site A's is inspection-verified** (`index.html` → `main.tsx` top-level `createRoot`) — stated asymmetrically because a boundary that wrapped nothing would pass all 9 tests. desktop 492/492 (+9) · 3/3 tsc passes · both reviewers 0 findings. Also: `tsconfig.testdom.json` gained `preload/api.d.ts` (the root-boundary test imports the real `App`). ⚠ **A jsdom landmine paid for once and documented in-file:** asserting a handler throw propagates synchronously out of `fireEvent.click` FAILS — jsdom reports listener exceptions to the global handler and React DEV re-dispatches as a DELAYED process-level unhandled exception that fails the whole suite while the test's own assertions stay green. _(was: the numbered home for tracked finding #35)_ (created 2026-07-29; see the note below on WHY it needed one)
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §11 (Electron Desktop UI), §16 (degrade-and-surface, never crash) · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** — · **Brief:** `docs/briefs/218-35-renderer-error-boundary.md`
**Files:** `apps/desktop/renderer/main.tsx` (root mount) · `apps/desktop/renderer/chrome/AppShell.tsx` (per-surface boundary) · a new `renderer/chrome/ErrorBoundary.tsx` · `apps/desktop/test-dom/`
⚠ **WHY THIS TASK EXISTS AS A NUMBER — a process finding worth keeping.** Finding #35 was tracked only as a bare `#35` in prose/handoffs, and **`scripts/spec-lint.sh` structurally cannot lint a brief for a bare-`#N` item**: its Task-ID check (`spec-lint.sh:84`) extracts `[A-Za-z]*[0-9]+\.[0-9]+` and then requires a matching `### N.M` tracker heading with an unticked box. So a bare-# finding can never carry a PASS stamp, and the mandatory pre-dispatch gate silently has no purchase on it. Per the Step-9 routing matrix this is the **INLINE-TARGET** treatment (*"a bare item in-scope for a phase is a task there, not an annotation"*), applied mechanically. ⇒ **Any tracked bare-`#N` finding that is about to be briefed needs a numbered home first.** (Sibling gap recorded in Carry-forward 6(a) — the spec-lint `[~]` fix owed to the scaffolding repo.)
`apps/desktop/renderer/main.tsx:6-12` mounts `<StrictMode><App /></StrictMode>` with **nothing wrapping it**, so a render-time throw anywhere unmounts everything `createRoot` owns. ⭐ **The codebase already documents its own gap** — `renderer/surfaces/copilot/Copilot.tsx:137-138` states verbatim *"There is NO ErrorBoundary anywhere in apps/desktop (verified), so React 18 unmounts the ENTIRE ROOT — not just the panel"*, and that comment is the stated justification for 9.34's `admitReply` validate-before-render gate. So one surface defended itself; the other ten did not.
⛔ **TWO candidate sites, and neither alone closes the finding:** `main.tsx:10` (covers everything incl. the `App.tsx:214` early-return Onboarding branch, but a caught error replaces the whole UI) vs `AppShell.tsx:480` around `{children}` (chrome/nav survive so the user can navigate away from a broken surface, but it covers **neither** Onboarding **nor** `App()` itself). **If only one ships, the uncovered crash class must be named here** — a partial fix recorded as complete is the L56 class.
⚠ **Async/handler failures are already handled** by the house fold-to-`{ok:false}` pattern (`renderer/lib/live.ts:206,254,289,326,365,401` + the sibling `lib/*.ts`); a boundary catches **render** throws only and does **not** supersede those folds. ⚠ **Rule-7:** the fallback must render no raw `error.message` and no stack — a caught error can carry worker-payload-derived content. ⚠ **No reporting channel exists:** `preload/bridge.ts:29-74` exposes 7 channels (allowlist `:101-109`, mirrored in `preload/inventory.json`) and **none is logging/telemetry** — persistent error reporting is a separate slice, not this one.
**Done-when:** a render throw renders a visible fallback with the root still mounted, the fallback leaks no raw message or stack, the happy path is behaviorally unchanged, the non-coverage of async/handler failures is pinned as characterization, and any uncovered crash class is named here. *(origin: 2026-07-29 — finding #35 promoted to a numbered task so it could be spec-linted)*

### 9.36 — The workspace read path re-gates the stored aggregate at the REPOSITORY boundary (the read-path dual of contracts L76)
- [x] **DONE** · `be62e348` · 2026-07-29 · **lead-ruled Option A + refinement** · **Brief:** `docs/briefs/223-9.36-workspace-read-regate.md` _(was: the numbered home for tracked finding `#39`)_
  **What landed:** the read gate at the repository boundary, both dialects, via a single-sourced `parseStoredWorkspace`/`parseStoredWorkspaceList` reusing `WorkspaceSchema.safeParse`; the new **`stored_row_schema_violation`** `DbErrorCode` (renamed from `referential_inconsistency` at review — the gate is the FULL schema, so a name describing one failure mode would send a future reader diagnosing a non-array allowlist hunting a foreign nested id; the referential pin is the **motivating case**, not the definition); permanently **non-retryable** in both boundary mappers; classified rather than collapsed at all three real consumers. `storeBackedWorkspacePosture` needed **no change** — it already failed closed on any `get()` error, so it was **regression-pinned, not edited** (the brief's "Modified" list was wrong; the implementer corrected it). `packages/db` 488/488 (+21), `apps/worker` 2025/2025, typecheck clean across 6 packages. Arch note: `fe4ad78d` (§4).
  ⭐ **Mechanism vs belt, recorded because a future reader will try to collapse them:** completeness is **by construction** (both nested JSON columns typed `unknown`, so an unparsed row cannot satisfy `Workspace` and a no-cast reader fails to compile) — **and the source census is NOT redundant with it.** Established **by experiment**: with the columns `unknown`, an explicit `row as Workspace` still compiles, and `as unknown as Workspace` compiles for **any** two types ⇒ **no purely structural TS mechanism can forbid a deliberate double-cast.** Unrepresentability closes the **accident**; the census is the **sole** coverage for a deliberate reintroduction. ⚠ **Named trade:** dropping `.$type<>()` also dropped a compile-time **write**-side check — accepted because it was redundant (`upsert`/`insertIfAbsent` take a `Workspace`) while the read-side type lie was load-bearing. **Do not restore the overlay.**
  ⚠ **Two-thirds of the lead's refinement, stated as such:** fail-closed ✅ · distinguishable at the boundary ✅ · **operator-visible ❌** — `boot.ts:578-592` flattens every `get()` fault into one value. **A capability, not a guarantee** (contracts L106). Surfacing leg = **task 9.38**, deliberately NOT in this slice.
  ⚠ **TDD deviation (forbidden-pattern #1), disclosed with residual AND remedy:** db-layer implementation preceded the dual-dialect tests. Mutation verification proved **discrimination**, not **spec-fidelity**. Discharged by a per-class **cross-map** against `packages/contracts/test/models/workspace.test.ts` — **another area's** independently-authored constraint enumeration (contracts **L107**): 9 classes newly covered, 2 already covered, `.strict()` unreachable via a stored row, and a JSON-literal-`null` case **handled by the gate but not independently pinned** (unbuildable through the typed write path). Two self-caught false passes banked as **L100**'s seventh shape — a `git ls-files` census blind to the slice's own untracked file, and a mutation the regex could not see.
**Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §4 (Operational Storage — repository contract, dual-dialect) · §5 (Policy/egress — the consumer whose assumption this makes true) · **Depends:** 9.30 (`615394a8`) · **Blocks:** `#38` (the revoke-path question shrinks once the read is gated)
**Files:** `packages/db/src/adapters/{sqlite,postgres}/index.ts` (6 cast sites) · `packages/db/src/repositories/interfaces.ts` (the typed error) · `packages/db/test/contract/repository-contract.test.ts` (dual-dialect) · consumers: `apps/worker/src/api/adapters/storeBackedWorkspacePosture.ts` · `apps/worker/src/composition/{egressRevoke,provisionWorkspace}.ts`
⚠ **WHY THIS TASK EXISTS AS A NUMBER** — same mechanism as 9.35: `#39` was tracked only as a bare `#N`, and `spec-lint.sh:84` requires a `### N.M` heading, so a bare-`#N` brief can never carry a PASS stamp. INLINE-TARGET treatment per the Step-9 matrix, applied mechanically.
⭐ **THE THREAT MODEL, and it is tighter than the framing this task was first scoped under. NO PRODUCTION WRITER CAN *ORIGINATE* A FOREIGN NESTED `workspaceId`:**

| Write site | Shape | Guard | Can originate? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `provisionWorkspace.ts:294` `insertIfAbsent` | whole-aggregate | `defaultWorkspace()` **parses** (`packages/contracts/src/models/workspace.ts:112`) **and wires both nested `workspaceId`s from `id`** (`:122`, `:129`); insert-only, no conflict-update | **No** — validated *and* foreign-by-construction impossible |
| `provisionWorkspace.ts:267` `updateProvisioningFields` | narrowed | `ProvisioningOwnedFields` makes a posture write untypeable (`:240-242`); values come from the already-parsed aggregate (`:264-266`) | **No** — the column is not written |
| `egressRevoke.ts:68` `upsert(revoked)` | **whole-aggregate** | get-before-upsert + only the ack fields change. **No parse** — `revoked = {...ws}` where `ws = existing.value`, an unchecked cast | **No — but it PRESERVES one** |

⇒ **Every surface in this task is an AMPLIFIER of a pre-existing out-of-band corruption, never an originator.** Origination stays sqlite-CLI-edit / restore-from-stale-snapshot only — **9.29's recorded accepted residual**. ⛔ **Do NOT let this become "a foreign `workspaceId` is undetected."** That is the quotable version and it is **false**.
⭐ **THE DECISIVE ARGUMENT IS NOT "one site is cheaper" — it is that a load-bearing comment in the egress path currently asserts a guarantee the code does not provide.** `packages/policy/src/workspace-policy.ts:7-10` and `:36` document `resolveWorkspacePolicy` as **total, needing no defensive deny, *because* "the referential pin … is already enforced by `WorkspaceSchema` upstream"** — in the layer feeding the §5 egress veto, route resolution, and admission. True for the create path; **false** for a value arriving via the unchecked cast. **Option A makes that documented assumption TRUE; a per-consumer fix leaves it false and compensates elsewhere** — prose asserting an untrue state, the defect class this round hit five separate times. ⚠ Bound it honestly: `resolveWorkspacePolicy` keys `workspaceId: w.id` (**top-level**), so the veto itself is not mis-keyed; it carries the sub-models **by reference**, so a consumer reading the *nested* id gets the foreign value — and `buildActivities.ts:854-861` is exactly that consumer, twice (`egressPolicy` and `providerMatrix`, three lines apart).
⛔ **SIX CAST SITES, THREE PER DIALECT — not the four first enumerated.** sqlite `:362` `get` · `:364` `list` · **`:412` `updateProvisioningFields`'s `.returning()` row** ‖ postgres `:383` `get` · `:385` `list` · **`:430` the same return.** The third-per-dialect surface is the narrowed *write* method handing back the **whole post-write row**, including the posture columns it did not write. `insertIfAbsent` is clean (returns a boolean, no cast). *(The four-site enumeration was stated **with its scope**, which is what made the fifth and sixth findable — contracts L100.)*
⛔ **THE REFINEMENT (lead ruling): fail closed with a DISTINGUISHABLE error, never a generic `store_fault`.** Policy/egress/admission consumers fail CLOSED on a referentially-inconsistent row (rules 4 + 5, non-negotiable). Read-only/diagnostic consumers must be able to report *"this workspace's stored posture is referentially inconsistent"* rather than crash — **a hard failure indistinguishable from an outage converts a latent inconsistency into an unexplainable one and removes the operator's ability to SEE the corruption they must repair.** Any repair/recovery path opts into a raw read **explicitly**, and is the **only** such caller — named, singular, and **pinned as singular**, else it becomes the new unchecked cast. Precedent to follow rather than invent: 7.19's `db_unavailable`-vs-`store_fault` work already established that a fault's CAUSE must be distinguishable from its terminal state.
**Done-when:** all six cast sites parse (or a stated-and-pinned subset, with the unparsed ones named here and justified); the typed error distinguishes referential-inconsistency from a store fault and from not-found; the dual-dialect repository-contract suite covers it on **both** adapters; the `workspace-policy.ts` comment is either true or amended in the same slice; any raw-read escape hatch is singular and pinned singular; and `storeBackedWorkspacePosture` no longer serves a corrupt policy. ⚠ **rule-5-adjacent ⇒ its OWN commit; `security-reviewer=invariant`; Step-9 → lead.** *(origin: 2026-07-29 — finding `#39` promoted to a numbered task; scope corrected twice before briefing, in both directions)*

### 9.38 — Surface the stored-row corruption code to a diagnostic consumer (the unmet third of 9.36's refinement)
- [x] DONE · `abf648c6` · 2026-07-30 · brief `docs/briefs/228-9.38-surface-the-stored-row-corruption-code.md` (`@fbbef3a1`) · **Depends:** 9.36 (`be62e348` — it produced the code this surfaces). ⭐ **Done-when verified against the COMMITTED TREE:** `corruptionSurface` mint present · `failClosedEgress` still on **every** failure branch (return byte-identical, the hard constraint) · fail-closed pin present · **BOTH** rule-7 markers (`name` **and** inside `egressPolicy`) · the no-unbounded-accumulation poll pin · and the reviewer-requested do-not-fix note on the deliberately-discarded `Result`. 8 new tests; `apps/worker` 2032 passed / 0 failed. **⭐ AND `#38` IS CLOSED BY A PIN, `64b682e5`** — a separate, test-only commit so its closure is bisectable after three sessions as a bare pointer. **The answer was not the expected one:** `egressRevoke` performs **no parse of its own and never did**, so nothing became redundant; what the question was really about is that its read cannot reach an unvalidated row, which now holds through 9.36's gate in **both** dialect adapters and is pinned against a **real `assembleBackends()` store**, paired with a **non-vacuity control** proving the same fixture sails through an *ungated* fake. ⛔ **`#38` ≠ task `9.38` — they collide only in digits** (handoff 019 maps 9.38 to bare id `#12`); resolved by tracing, not by guessing, per 6(a5). ⚠ **RESIDUAL, DISCLOSED NOT SILENT:** a thrown/transient **outage still mints NO item**, so absence-of-item continues to conflate *healthy* with *store outage*. Pre-existing — outages were already invisible through `egressStatus` — **not worsened, and declined for a real reason rather than a scheduling one: there is no reliable outage signal to key on without risking a FALSE corruption report**, which would trade a true-positive guarantee for a false-positive risk. ⇒ Lesson **L114** banked from this slice (`d566a5f1`): *a safe default returned with a success flag destroys the cause at that frame* — ⛔ **not L106, and the remedies do not transfer.**
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §4 (Operational Storage — the read contract that produces the code) · §16 (System Health, degrade-and-surface) · **Depends:** 9.36 · **Blocks:** —
**Files:** `apps/worker/src/boot.ts` (`health.egressStatus`, ~`:578-592`) and/or the System Health projection · tests
The lead's Option-A refinement for 9.36 had **three** parts. Two landed: policy/egress consumers **fail closed** on a corrupt stored row ✅, and the error is **distinguishable at the repository boundary** (`stored_row_schema_violation`, permanently non-retryable, classified at all three consumers) ✅. **The third did not: no diagnostic consumer can REPORT it.** Verified at 9.36's Step 2.5 — `boot.ts`'s `egressStatus` folds **any** `get()` error, including this one, into the same generic fail-closed value, so a corrupt row is **indistinguishable from `not_found` or a plain outage**. ⇒ **A capability, not a guarantee** (contracts **L106**): a correctly-typed, correctly-fail-closed error that reaches no consumer is indistinguishable from the failure it was created to be distinguishable from. **The operator cannot see the corruption they must repair.**
⛔ **HARD CONSTRAINT: fail-closed must NOT be weakened to achieve visibility.** The fail-closed direction is correct and is not the defect. An **absent/empty** state must remain distinguishable from a **corrupt** one. Code-only per rule 7 — never raw policy content.
**Done-when:** a corrupt stored row produces a distinct, code-only surfaced state (System Health item and/or a distinct `egressStatus` field) that an operator can act on; the fail-closed behaviour of every policy/egress consumer is unchanged and pinned as such; and empty-vs-corrupt remain distinguishable. *(origin: 2026-07-29 9.36 Step-2.5, worker-implementer Q3 verification; materialized here at the close-out because it had existed only as harness task metadata — session-scoped and lost at respawn, contracts L51)*

**Files:** `apps/desktop/renderer/surfaces/copilot/Copilot.tsx` — cited **BY SYMBOL** per L101/L69, positions recorded as DATA as of `3b073420` and not as the citation: the `CopilotTurnSeed` interface (`:86`), the `readonly turns?` prop on `CopilotProps` (`:112`), its destructuring as `seedTurns` (`:288`), and the door-2 seed initialisation `seedTurns.map((t) => ({...t, reply: admitReply(t.reply)}))` (`:294`) · `apps/desktop/test-dom/copilot-panel.test.tsx` (the only non-scanner consumer) · `apps/desktop/test/renderer/copilot-rehydrate-seam.test.ts` (9.25's scanner — disposition is a Step-2.5 question)
⭐ **This is L103 applied to the one case 9.25 proved needed it.** 9.25 shipped a 4-test scanner as an explicit **backstop** after three adversarial rounds, each closing a **demonstrated** evasion (JSX spread · aliased import / namespace tag / `createElement` · barrel re-export indirection), and stopped with two residuals **named rather than closed**. Its own durable reading: *"no production consumer" is a negative claim over an UNBOUNDED construction space, so a scanner over it is a detector, not a gate* — and *"if this ever must be airtight, the structural answer is: DELETE the seed door and construct turns another way in tests."* **Deleting the prop converts an open-space detector into a closed-space gate: with no `turns` prop there is nothing to police, and both named residuals become unreachable rather than accepted.**
⛔ **The risk removed is a rule-5 one, so state it precisely: this does NOT solve disclosure-on-rehydrate.** 9.25 established that the fail-open-by-omission case is **unreachable today**, which is a different claim from *handled*. Deleting the seed door removes the **construction route**; it does not create derived disclosure state. ⇒ **A future restore path still owes 9.25's precondition in full — the obligation MOVES to the restore producer, it does not discharge.**
⭐ **Verified before briefing, so the slice is not authored on a guess:** `admitReply` has **three** call sites — `:160` (the once-minted branded failure reply), `:294` (door 2, deleted here), `:338` (door 1, the live-ask path). **Deleting door 2 leaves door 1 and the failure mint intact, so 9.26's client-side re-gate is preserved** and `admitReply` keeps live callers regardless (it is also the sole minter of 9.34's `AdmittedCopilotAnswer` brand). ⚠ **Consequence that must not be missed:** `Copilot.tsx`'s own comments at `:22`, `:82`, `:110`, `:291-292` and `:335-336` describe *"both doors"* / *"door 2"* / *"the seed prop is door 2"* — **deleting the prop makes every one of them false.** A file whose comments describe a prop that no longer exists is the defect 13.19 correctly fixed for its own stale comment; it is an acceptance bullet here, not a nicety.
**Done-when:** `CopilotProps` has no `turns` member and `CopilotTurnSeed` is gone; the live-ask path, `admitReply` door 1, the branded-failure mint and 9.24's `ASK_FAILED` rendering are all unchanged and pinned green; the `test-dom` consumer builds turn state **without** a mount-time seed; every *"door 2" / "both doors"* comment is corrected rather than left asserting a deleted prop; the 9.25 scanner's disposition is decided per the brief's Step-2.5 question and is **not** left policing a prop that cannot exist; and 9.25's dormant-precondition status is restated as **moved to the restore producer**, not closed. *(origin: 2026-07-29 9.25 "recorded as shape; not done"; numbered 2026-07-30 so it could be spec-linted and briefed — per Carry-forward 6(a5)(i), a bare pointer is not a specification)*

### 9.41 — Audit-link drill-down (the 9.10-D sub-arc, given a first-class heading): contracts summary shape → worker resolver → desktop affordance
- [~] PARTIAL · landed: **leg A** `bd73d4ea` 2026-07-30 (contract; brief `docs/briefs/229-9.41-A-audit-drill-summary-shape.md` `@0b1d7523`) — `UiSafeAuditDrillSummary` + `.strict()` schema + `_uiSafeParity` entry + sorted `UI_SAFE_ALLOWLIST.auditDrillSummary`, carrying `event` + `occurredAt` only; **remaining:** leg **B** (worker — the drill-down resolver, `globalDrillDown` precedent) then leg **C** (desktop — the Recent Changes affordance at `Today.tsx:217-218`). ⭐ **Done-when for A verified against the committed tree:** type present, allowlist entry present, and the schema uses **`uiSafeToken`, not a raw string**. **Exclusion pinned TWICE on purpose** — an allowlist-array check documenting the seven forbidden names, plus `.strict()` rejection with each of `auditRef`/`actor`/`refs`/`payloadHash`/`beforeSummary`/`afterSummary`/`workspaceId` injected **individually** rather than batched, which is what makes it discriminating. Security review clean; code-quality 1 low fixed in-slice. `packages/contracts` 785 green, `packages/domain` 307 green untouched.
⭐ **TWO FINDINGS FROM LEG A, both refuting something written by the orchestrator:** **(i)** the brief required confirming *"`registry-all` stays green with the new schema registered"* — but `registry-all.test.ts` scopes only `schemas/*.schema.json` and `*_SCHEMA_ID` barrel exports, and `api/ui-safe.ts` produces **neither**, so it is invisible to ui-safe additions **by construction** and would have stayed green whether or not any work was done. **The bullet was itself a false-green and is WITHDRAWN, not waived.** Established by three scoped negatives: 29 schema files all Appendix-A with zero ui-safe names · all 30 `emitJsonSchema` call sites in `src/models/` or `src/provider/` · 32 model snapshots all Appendix-A. ⇒ **ui-safe types have their OWN lighter convention** — interface + `.strict()` schema + `_uiSafeParity` tuple + sorted allowlist entry + a `PROJECTIONS`-table row, **and that table IS the snapshot.** **(ii)** ⛔ **`AuditRecord.event` is an OPEN `z.string().min(1)`** carrying an explicit `arch_gap` comment (taxonomy unspecified upstream, deliberately not an enum) — so **a naive reuse would have given a brand-new UI-safe surface an UNBOUNDED string inherited from an `arch_gap` field.** Bounded instead with the shared `uiSafeToken` (≤64), and security **checked real call-site values rather than assuming the bound fits** (19–38 chars, fails closed not open). ⇒ lesson banked at L114's neighbour slot when next routed.
⭐ **PARITY IS FULLY THREE-WAY, NO UNPINNED EDGE — checked because the orchestrator suspected a gap, and there isn't one; recording it CLOSED so nobody inherits a phantom doubt (L111).** The schema is the pivot: `_uiSafeParity` pins interface↔schema at **compile** time (an interface field the schema does not infer fails `tsc`), and the `PROJECTIONS` freeze test pins schema↔allowlist at **runtime**. Both holding simultaneously means interface and allowlist cannot drift without first breaking one of them. **True for all 17 ui-safe entries, not just the new one.**

## Phase 10 — Cross-cutting concerns (observability, redaction, supervision, degraded modes, backup/recovery, config/time)
**Goal:** Build the production-grade cross-cutting substrate every subsystem depends on — non-bypassable structured-logging + mandatory redaction, a typed System Health surface per failure class, the worker-supervision lifecycle, Temporal-unavailable / Keychain-locked degraded modes, operational-truth backup/restore, and config/time conventions — as first-class invariants, ordered invariants → lifecycle → tests, not deferred hardening. · **Spec anchors:** §16 (Observability/redaction; Worker supervision & lifecycle; Backup & recovery; Configuration & time; Error-handling); cross-refs §4, §5, §8, §9, §13; OBS-1/OBS-2, LIFE-1/4/5/6, REQ-NF-001/004/006, REQ-S-002/003, REQ-D-002/004 · **Track:** eval-security · **Depends on (phases):** 2, 7
**Gate:** ✅ /phase-exit 10 CLEAR (2026-07-02) — checklist: docs/archive/IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md Part 3 · audits: docs/audits/phase10-arch-drift.md, phase10-security.md, phase10-reachability.md

### 10.1 — Mandatory redaction layer + structured logger (non-bypassable security invariant)
- [x] DONE · `9fd682a6` · 2026-07-02
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §16 Observability/structured-logging/redaction; REQ-S-003 (secrets/raw never logged), REQ-S-002 (Employer-Work raw never logged) · **Depends:** none · **Blocks:** 10.8
**Files:** packages/contracts/src/observability/log-record.ts; packages/domain/src/redaction/redact.ts + redaction-rules.ts; apps/worker/src/observability/logger.ts
Structured records carry correlation ID + workflowRunId (+ workspaceId where present); the logger is the SINGLE chokepoint — no path reaches a sink without first passing redaction. Redaction runs BEFORE any sink, stripping credential-shaped strings and §16 raw-content fields (provider prompts, raw Employer-Work content, AgentResult.logs), never emitted at default level; raw is reachable only behind an explicit non-default debug flag, and Employer-Work raw stays redacted even then (§5). Fail-safe by allowlist of known-safe fields (denylist insufficient) — an unclassifiable field defaults to redacted; coverage includes thrown Errors / exception messages / stack traces. Redacted output is structurally stable (typed marker e.g. [REDACTED:credential]); pure rules live in domain (no I/O), unit-tested against a secret-fixture corpus.

### 10.2 — Error-handling convention: typed Result + failure taxonomy + nothing-fails-silently routing
- [x] DONE · `9fd682a6` · 2026-07-02
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §16 Error-handling convention (typed Result + failure taxonomy + nothing-fails-silently routing) · **Depends:** none · **Blocks:** 10.3, 10.5, 10.7
**Files:** packages/contracts/src/primitives/result.ts + failure.ts; packages/domain/src/error-routing/route-failure.ts
Every cross-subsystem operation returns a typed Result with an enumerated failure-variant taxonomy (validation_rejected, provider_failed, budget_exceeded, connector_unreachable, write_conflict, schema_rejected, degraded_unavailable, …) — failures are values, never thrown-and-swallowed. A canonical routing function maps each variant → {outbox?, healthClass?, retryable}; there is no path that neither retries nor surfaces — 'nothing fails silently' is enforced by the routing function, exhaustive over FailureVariant (never a default fallthrough), not by convention. The failure-class enum stays in sync with the OBS-2 classes (10.3) and degraded modes (10.5) that discriminate on it; routing is pure/deterministic and unit-testable without I/O. (routeFailure is reachable — called by degraded/keychain-locked + temporal-unavailable.)

### 10.3 — System Health surface: typed per-failure-class items, persistent, audit-linked, deduped (OBS-1/OBS-2)
- [x] DONE · `70c8caff` · 2026-07-02
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §16 System Health + §9 workflow 11 surfacing; OBS-1/OBS-2 · **Depends:** 10.2, P2, P7 · **Blocks:** 10.4, 10.5, 10.6, 10.8
**Files:** packages/contracts/src/models/health-item.ts (HealthItem + FailureClass enum); packages/db/src/schema/health-items.ts (+ pg variant, migration); apps/worker/src/health/surface.ts
Each OBS-2 failure class produces a DISTINCT typed HealthItem (connector outage, failed/blocked write-through, budget breach, missed/late schedule, schema rejection) — discriminated, not a single generic item; OBS-1 persistent surfaces (connector/GBrain health, run status, queue/outbox depth, sync lag, agent cost, approval backlog) are modeled as read-model state. Every item is AUDIT-LINKED (ref to its AuditRecord) and PERSISTENT until resolved/acknowledged, stored durably in the operational store so acknowledgement survives worker restart and read-model rebuild. Surfacing is idempotent — dedupe by (failureClass, subjectRef); re-occurrence updates lastSeen/occurrenceCount instead of spawning duplicates. Lifecycle open → acknowledged | resolved (terminal), with auto-resolve when the underlying condition clears; the failure-class discriminant set is a checked-in type-shape contract so cross-track consumers cannot drift.

### 10.4 — Worker supervision contract: restart + bounded backoff, crash-loop → worker-down, lease re-acquire, in-flight recovery
- [x] DONE · `2a544800` · 2026-07-02 · dormant
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §16 Worker supervision & lifecycle + §8 external-write envelope + §9 LIFE-3 resume; REQ-NF-006 · **Depends:** 10.3, P7 · **Blocks:** 10.5, 10.8
**Files:** apps/desktop/main/worker-supervisor.ts (spawn, backoff, crash-loop threshold, worker-down state); apps/worker/src/lifecycle/lease-reacquire.ts + supervision-policy.ts + recovery.ts
Electron main supervises the worker: on crash it restarts with bounded exponential backoff (redacted-logged); a crash-loop threshold (N restarts in a window) STOPS the respawn loop and surfaces a distinct worker-down health item + UI state instead of looping forever. On respawn the worker re-acquires the LIFE-1 single-instance lease — if a live instance holds it the new worker does NOT double-run (no two owners of the operational store / GBrain files). In-flight side effects recover via Temporal resume (LIFE-3) reusing the §8 external-write envelope so an interrupted side effect is NOT duplicated (replay matches receipt/canonical key); recovery is idempotent + re-drivable, and a failure to recover surfaces a typed item rather than silently dropping the run. Restart-survival IS wired (supervisor backoff/fork, reachable); the idempotent-resume half (recoverRun) has no production caller yet (evals-only) and the composition root still binds the in-memory store — production wiring of the recovery/lease half is deferred to the Phase 11 app-shell wave (UNREACHABLE-BY-DESIGN, bounded, gate-accepted).

### 10.5 — Degraded modes: Temporal-unavailable and Keychain-locked as first-class states
- [x] DONE · `2a544800` · 2026-07-02 · dormant
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §16 degraded modes (Temporal-unavailable + Keychain-locked); LIFE-6 wake hooks; routed through 10.2/10.3 · **Depends:** 10.2, 10.3, 10.4, P7 · **Blocks:** 10.8
**Files:** apps/worker/src/lifecycle/degraded/temporal-unavailable.ts + keychain-locked.ts
Temporal-unavailable BLOCKS workflow dispatch (queue/hold, never silently drop triggers), surfaces a distinct deduped worker_down item via routeFailure(degraded_unavailable), retries with bounded backoff, and auto-clears + drains on reconnect. Keychain-locked/denied marks affected providers/connectors DEGRADED and holds dependent jobs as retryable (not failed_terminal, so no work is lost), re-attempting on unlock via LIFE-6 wake/power hooks. Both are typed degraded states routed through the 10.2 taxonomy + 10.3 surface, not ad-hoc exceptions; re-attempt-on-unlock / reconnect is idempotent through the normal admission/envelope path (no duplicated side effects). Controllers built + conformance-green; production wiring into worker boot is deferred to the Phase 11 app-shell wave.

Local Ollama/LM Studio are an OPTIONAL zero-egress path, never a release gate.

### 12.6 — KnowledgeWriter ownership/merge/secret + human-section + out-of-band-reconcile suite
- [x] DONE · `aecb8f1e` · 2026-07-01
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §12; §6; KN-7/KN-8 · **Depends:** P6 · **Blocks:** —

Human-owned section preservation (KN-7): an overwrite of a human-owned region is REJECTED and audited; assistant regions are bounded by explicit start/end markers with stable IDs (KN-8) and merges stay inside them. A blocking secret scan REJECTS (does not redact) a credential-shaped plan — the commit never lands; atomic write with compare-revision precondition fails a stale base rather than clobbering. Out-of-band reconciliation (Obsidian Sync/iCloud/git): a clean external change advances the base revision, a conflicting concurrent change produces a System Health conflict-review item (NOT a silent lost update), pending KW writes apply before queued GBrain jobs on wake; GBrain re-index fires AFTER the commit, async + idempotent, and its failure never rolls back the commit. (Authored during Phase 4/§6 work — aecb8f1e, 11801362.)

A maxRuntimeSeconds or maxCostUsd breach CANCELS the job, records it in audit, and surfaces a System Health item (OBS-2). The Broker applies a configurable DEFAULT cap to an LLM-calling job lacking one (COST-2); an uncapped job is never run uncapped. AgentJob terminal state is `cancelled_budget`, distinct from failed_retryable/failed_terminal.
