#### Residuals (12)
- **⛔ THE 24.6-A GUARD SWEEP'S ITERATION-3 FINDINGS: 7 of 7 VERIFIED, ALL SURVIVE (task #41 DONE 2026-07-29, evalsec).** They entered the round labelled *leads, not findings* — none simulation-proven. **All seven are now confirmed real**, five by mutation window (mutate → run → revert → `git status` clean), two by reading where there is no logic to mutate and the ABSENCE is the finding. ⛔ **RECORDED, NOT FIXED** — deliberate, under the owner's teardown boundary (a recorded finding serves the next session as well as a fixed one, and none of these is a LIVE safety defect: they are guards blind to FUTURE regressions plus two false-coverage claims). Per item, with method:
  - **F1** (simulated) — `resolveEnforcedBudget` (`packages/providers`) mutated to ignore job-explicit caps and always return `defaults.global` (60s vs the job's 300s): `suites/budget-cap` **8/8 still green**. No fixture has usage between 60–300s, so nothing discriminates. ⚠ Doc 124's baseline of "7" was **stale** — it is 8.
  - **F2** (simulated) — a hostile `rawPrompt?` field added to the REAL `HealthFailure` interface (`apps/worker/src/health/surface.ts`): `suites/system-health` **11/11 still green**. The test's `safeInputKeys` is a hand-typed literal never derived from the real type, so it **structurally cannot** notice a field addition. An L74 emptiable-label instance in a different costume.
  - **F3** (simulated) — the entire cost-breach branch of `detectBudgetBreach` disabled: **8/8 still green**. No fixture ever varies `costUsd` off `0`, so the cost dimension does zero work.
  - **F4** (simulated) — a fake leaked secret appended to `breachAudit`'s `afterSummary`: **8/8 still green**. `toContain` cannot fail on EXTRA content — the rule-7 assertion is one-directional.
  - **F5** (simulated, in-package) — the auth-suite's `rejected && untouched` fold weakened to `rejected` alone: **4/4 identical**. The current `REJECT_VECTORS` never drive a touched-port-while-rejected scenario, so the `untouched` conjunct currently discriminates nothing (an L79 instance: the conjunct's fixtures never reach it).
  - **F6** (verified by reading — no channel logic exists to mutate; the absence IS the finding) — the approval-flow suite's describe name claims **cross-channel** coverage, but `"channel"` appears only in prose, describe text, and a variable name (`secondChannelNoop`); every assertion calls the pure, **2-arg, channel-agnostic** `approvalMachine.transition(from,to)`. The real cross-channel exactly-once guarantee is exercised by a SIBLING suite (`exactly-once-suite.ts`, real Mac/Telegram through the command router — #40's suite). ⛔ **An L82 false-coverage claim**; recording it is L82's legitimate "withdraw + record" disposition, not the forbidden re-point.
  - **F7** (verified by reading) — `crossCalendarScheduling.ts:302-308` branches on `!isOk(gathered)` only and never inspects `.error.code` (it merely interpolates it into an unasserted message). So calendar-conflict fixture case 1 (`calendar_unreachable`) and case 3 (`gate_rejected`) hit the **byte-identical** branch; only case 2 (partial/missing-source, `:314-320`) is genuinely distinct. **3 fixtures, 2 real paths.**
  ⛔ **LABEL CHANGE, STATED EXPLICITLY BECAUSE THE DISTINCTION WAS CAREFULLY MAINTAINED AND MUST NOT BE LOST NOW THAT IT MOVED IN THE SAFE DIRECTION: these seven are UPGRADED FROM "LEAD" TO "VERIFIED FINDING."** They entered the round as leads precisely because none was proven; they are now proven. ⭐ **AND THE HIT RATE IS THE POINT: 7 of 7 leads verified — 100%.** A lens that converts every lead into a confirmed finding is **strong evidence the sweep's remaining lenses hold more**, which makes #30's *"terminated on an owner bound, not on dryness"* a far sharper warning than it was when first written. Whoever resumes should know the sample was **not merely incomplete but reliably productive**. (Had these been reported as findings originally, they would have been an overclaim that happened to come true — which teaches nothing; the label discipline is what makes this legible.)
  **Two further F1-class round-trip assertions in `suites/budget-cap/budget-cap.test.ts`, verdicts kept at their separate strengths:**
  - **RT1 — REAL GAP** (`:182`): `expect(breach.runtime).toEqual({observed:900, limit: ENFORCED.maxRuntimeSeconds})` compares `limit` against **the same module-level `ENFORCED` the SUT itself consumes**, not an independent literal (`300`). Already covered by the F1 window: with `resolveEnforcedBudget` mutated to always return `defaults.global`, `ENFORCED.maxRuntimeSeconds` recomputes to the same wrong `60` on **both** sides, so the self-comparison **cannot fail regardless of correctness**. Nothing in the file independently pins the 300s cap.
  - **RT2 — WEAK/REDUNDANT IN ISOLATION, but NOT a suite-level blind spot** (`:226-227`): the re-drive test only compares `b.error.branch` to `a.error.branch`. Mutating the live literal (`broker.ts:377`) gave **7 PASS / 1 FAIL** — `budget-cap.test.ts:149` independently pins `branch` and caught it; only the re-drive test stayed green, since an a↔b comparison cannot see a regression both invocations share. ⚠ *"Weak assertion"* and *"blind suite"* are different claims; they are deliberately not collapsed here.
  - ⛔ **NEW INCIDENTAL FINDING — a DEAD COMPUTED FIELD, and it is a live drift risk:** `budget-enforcer.ts` computes a `branch` field on its `GateDeny` that **`broker.ts:377` discards entirely, re-hardcoding its own `"cancelled_budget"` literal**. Two independent literals that merely happen to agree today. It is also *why* a plausible mutation target can be unreachable — see the L75 amendment.
  ⚠ **PROCESS NOTE WORTH KEEPING (self-caught by the implementer):** the first RT2 result was reported **before the command was run** and was wrong (claimed 7/1; the real run of that target was 8/0), because the mutation hit the dead `budget-enforcer.ts` field rather than the live `broker.ts` literal — **L75's own trap, hit inside the technique L75 defines.** They flagged their own error, re-ran, and corrected before it was acted on. Recorded because self-correction at this speed is the behaviour that keeps a verified-findings list trustworthy.

#### 13.8a — EntityResolver (source entity → canonical vault note path, WS-8)
**State:** DONE · `c3d62436` · 2026-07-25 (ground-before-write EntityResolver: resolve/create-stub/withhold; WS-8-scoped read + re-gate + foreign-candidate drop; pure/never-throws/fail-closed-to-unresolved; unblocks 13.8b/c/d/f/g + 13.14) · **Kind:** build · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10), §5 (WS-8) · **Depends:** P4 (GBrain adapter/index), 13.3 (retrieval) · **Blocks:** 13.8c, 13.8d, 13.14

Resolve a referenced entity (person/project/concept) to an EXISTING canonical note path via a workspace-scoped GBrain read (WS-8 — no cross-brain query, safety rule 4), returning a resolved path OR a "create-stub" decision — the governed analog of osb's "ground the path before writing; a synthesis-named path is never proof the note exists". Deterministic given the GBrain read: exact-slug/alias match resolves; ambiguous (2+) or lossy → withhold + flag (never a fabricated path). PURE over an injected GBrain read port; never-throws; fail-closed to "unresolved" on any fault. This is the net-new load-bearing gap (no resolver exists today). **Done-when:** the resolver maps a hit to its real path, withholds on ambiguity, and a WS-8 test proves it never resolves across workspaces.

#### 13.8b — LinkHealer + governed auto-link LinkMutations (deterministic)
**State:** DONE · `262df7b8` · 2026-07-25 (LinkHealer: faithful-match forward-link heal → LinkMutation→KW; lossy/fuzzy/2+ withhold; NO backlinks; WS-8 foreign-drop; pure/never-throws; shared `synthesis/match-keys.ts` with the resolver — predicate-lives-once) · **Kind:** build · **Spec:** §6 (KN-11) · **Depends:** 13.8a, P4 (KnowledgeWriter `applyLink`) · **Blocks:** 13.8c

The governed port of osb's heal_links.py: given a note's Title→slug references, emit corrective `LinkMutation{op:'add',srcPath,dstSlug}` ONLY on a faithful/unambiguous slug match (fold em/en dashes; do NOT flatten spaces↔hyphens; a lossy `C++`→`c`, fuzzy, or 2+-candidate match is withheld to a proposed clarification, never auto-healed). Backlinks are NOT authored — derived read-only via GBrain `get_backlinks` (KN-11). PURE, deterministic, never-throws; every emitted mutation flows through the KMP → KnowledgeWriter (never a direct write). **Done-when:** a faithful Title→kebab link heals via a LinkMutation, an ambiguous one is withheld, and no backlink section is ever authored.

#### 13.8h — Cap the MODEL-supplied `candidate.entityRefs` fan-out in `planSynthesis` (unbounded-read vector)
**State:** DONE · `bed423cb` · 2026-07-29 — ⛔ **this arming precondition is CLOSED (see §ARM-RESEARCH item 8)** · **Kind:** build · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10) · **Depends:** 13.8c (`461a0186`) · **Blocks:** the 13.8e/13.8f arming crossing
⛔ **DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE FROM THIS TASK'S OWN `Done-when` — READ BEFORE "FIXING" IT.** The Done-when below says *"keep the cap a single shared constant so a caller-side and planner-side cap can't drift."* **That was overridden on purpose** (orchestrator ruling at Step 2.5, 2026-07-29): the shipped cap is `MAX_MODEL_ENTITY_REFS`, an **INDEPENDENT** constant in `planner.ts`, deliberately NOT coupled to `meeting-rewrite.ts`'s `MAX_ENTITY_REFS`. **Reason:** single-sourcing (L5/L37) governs literals that must *agree*; these must not. `MAX_ENTITY_REFS` bounds **deterministic input the meeting path owns**; `MAX_MODEL_ENTITY_REFS` bounds **adversarial model output**. Coupled, a future tuning of one silently retunes the other's **threat model** — and a circular import (`meeting-rewrite` → `planner`) made sharing impossible anyway. Both are 200 today so nothing changed behaviourally, and the in-code comment states the decoupling. ⛔ **Do not "helpfully" single-source them.**
⭐ **Surfaced, not silently sliced:** `SynthesisOutcome.entityRefsTruncated` carries the dropped **count** (rule 7 — a count, never a ref/name/path), hoisted above the `try` so a later fault cannot discard it (mirroring 13.8m-A's refusals accumulator — the first time this round that invariant was pinned *before* a mutation pass found it missing). The truncation is head-first and deterministic, pinned by asserting the **actual GBrain call count** rather than the reported number — the property, not the mechanism (L70).
**Files:** `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/planner.ts` (`collectEntities` — resolves `candidate.entityRefs` with no cap) · `test/synthesis-planner.test.ts`
`planSynthesis` resolves the MODEL-supplied `candidate.entityRefs` with **no cap**, so a degenerate REASON output (a model emitting hundreds/thousands of refs) drives an **unbounded sequential GBrain read loop** — a self-inflicted resource-exhaustion path one layer BELOW the deterministic caps 13.8d/13.8f-A enforce on the inputs they own. Those callers cannot fix it: they bound their own `input.entityRefs`, not the planner's model-derived set. Currently invisible because the planner is dormant — which is exactly why it must be closed BEFORE the synthesis path arms, not after. Cap the model-derived fan-out (mirror `MAX_ENTITY_REFS`), surface the truncation rather than dropping silently, and keep the cap a single shared constant so a caller-side and planner-side cap can't drift. **Done-when:** a degenerate candidate with refs beyond the cap issues a bounded number of GBrain reads, the truncation is observable (not a silent slice), and the cap constant is shared with the deterministic callers. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8f-A Step-9 flag, knowledge — reviewer-surfaced)*

#### 13.8j — Namespace entity stub paths so untrusted names cannot collide with KN-12 structural surfaces
**State:** DONE · `6dc6c34f` · 2026-07-26 (per-kind namespace `people/`/`projects/`/`concepts/` + `entities/` fail-safe for a malformed model-supplied kind; derivation lives ONCE in `stubNotePathFor`; `ReadonlyMap` not an object literal, after the first fix let `__proto__`/`toString` resolve through the prototype chain back to the vault root — contracts L65; 16 hostile-kind vectors pinned; exact-match, no normalization) · **Kind:** build · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10 grounding, KN-12 structural parity), safety rule 1 · **Depends:** 13.8c `461a0186`, 13.8f-A `d723a4fc`, 13.8g-A · **Blocks:** the 13.8e/13.8f arming crossing
**Files:** `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/entity-resolver.ts` (shared path derivation) · `planner.ts:288` · `meeting-rewrite.ts:185` · tests
Entity stub paths are minted at the VAULT ROOT as `${proposedSlug}.md` at **two** independent call sites — `meeting-rewrite.ts:185` AND `planner.ts:288` (the orchestrator's verification found the second; the Step-9 report named only the first, so **the SOURCE-ingestion path carries it too**, and that path is already bound dormant by 13.8d `172f9aed`). So an entity named `Index`/`Log`/`README` mints `index.md`/`log.md` — the KnowledgeWriter-owned KN-12 structural surfaces (navigation catalog + append-only op-log). This defeats a deliberate design decision: `MeetingRewriteDeps` omits the `structural` port precisely so a meeting cannot touch those files, and this reaches them by another door. Attacker-reachable since 13.8g-A began feeding entity names from untrusted attendee strings. Traversal itself is clean (`entitySlug` collapses `../../etc/passwd` → `etc-passwd`); the exposure is COLLISION, not escape. **⛔ Fix by NAMESPACE (`people/`/`projects/`/`concepts/` by `EntityKind`), NOT a reserved-slug denylist** — enumeration is structurally unwinnable and this codebase has paid for it twice (the shadow-env key set needed 3 re-grounds and still missed a switch, worker L72; the settings-injection FIELD enumeration was retired for a presence-degrade, 18.39-B). Derive the path in ONE shared place so both consumers inherit the namespace by construction — the duplication is what enabled the defect. **Done-when:** an `Index`/`Log`-named entity cannot mint a root structural file via EITHER path, stub paths are namespaced per kind, resolved (non-stub) paths are unchanged, and no inline `${...proposedSlug}.md` construction remains. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8g-A Step-9 flag, knowledge — both reviewers; scope widened by orchestrator verification)*

#### 13.8k — INVARIANT: every path entering `groundedPaths` is shape-validated, whoever produced it
**State:** DONE · `3acb2e0b` · 2026-07-26 (shared `admitGroundedPath` as the single admission point; resolver calls it so every consumer inherits it; owned-surface set DERIVED from exported `structural-files.ts` constants; refusal withholds with a code-only reason, never sanitizes; resolved paths byte-identical. 3 review catches fixed in-slice incl. an already-grounded regression that minted a NoteCreate over a confirmed note, and a 4-read TOCTOU on `meetingNotePath`; 2 of its own new pins were unsound and are now mutation-verified) — ⛔ **arming precondition for the synthesis path** · **Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10 grounding, KN-12), §5 (WS-8), safety rule 1 · **Depends:** 13.8j · **Blocks:** the 13.8e/13.8f arming crossing
**Files:** `packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/entity-resolver.ts` (the `resolved` branch / candidate-row admission) · tests
13.8j closed the STUB-minting door to the writer-owned KN-12 structural surfaces by namespacing. The **`resolved` branch is a third door it deliberately did not touch:** `resolveEntity` returns `candidate.path` **VERBATIM** from the GBrain read, shape-guarded only as a non-empty string — so a poisoned candidate row carrying `path: "index.md"` (or `log.md`, or a `Logs/<date>.md`) plus a faithfully-matching title RESOLVES to that path, lands in `groundedPaths`, and the model may then patch it. 13.8j is CORRECT not to re-path resolved notes (re-pathing would silently break every grounding match — pinned by `resolved_paths_unchanged`), so the fix belongs at the resolver's admission boundary instead: reject/withhold a candidate row whose `path` is shape-invalid or targets a writer-owned structural surface, rather than sanitizing it after the fact. Note the general shape — **13.8j fixed the instances of a CONSTRUCTION; this is a different route to the same INVARIANT violation** (contracts L64: enumerate the concept, not the construction). **⚠ SCOPED AS AN INVARIANT, deliberately (2026-07-26, on the implementer's correction):** the first phrasing of this task — "add a path-shape guard on candidate rows at the resolver boundary" — was itself a CONSTRUCTION/location phrasing, i.e. the same L64 error one layer up: it would be satisfied by guarding the resolver while leaving any future producer free to put an unvalidated path into `groundedPaths` by another route. The requirement is the INVARIANT: **every path that enters `groundedPaths` is shape-validated, regardless of which code produced it** — resolved candidate rows today, and any future producer by construction. Implement it where that invariant can be enforced once (the point where paths enter the grounded set), not at each known producer. **THREE routes to this one invariant are now known** (a strong argument that the invariant, not the guard site, is the right unit): (i) stub minting — closed by 13.8j's namespace construction; (ii) RESOLVED candidate rows — `candidate.path` verbatim from the GBrain read (the reported route); (iii) **`meetingNotePath`** — caller-supplied and SEEDS the grounded set (`meeting-rewrite.ts:172`), so a meeting note "at" `index.md` lets the model patch the navigation catalog; found at Step 2.5 by applying the invariant framing, enumerated by neither the Finding nor the brief. ⚠ Requires a WRITE to `markdown-vault/structural-files.ts`: it exports no owned-surface constants today (index path is a caller arg; `logsDir`/`pointerPath` are inline defaults), so the guard has nothing to derive from until `STRUCTURAL_INDEX_PATH`/`STRUCTURAL_LOG_POINTER_PATH`/`STRUCTURAL_LOGS_DIR` are exported and consumed by the builders as their defaults. Residual: those being DEFAULTS means a caller could pass a custom path the guard wouldn't know — closed by a structural pin that no production call site does, rather than by removing configurability. **Done-when:** the invariant holds and is pinned — a path targeting `index.md`/`log.md`/`Logs/…` or otherwise shape-invalid cannot enter `groundedPaths` from ANY producer; legitimate resolved paths are untouched (grounding matches still work); and the enforcement lives in ONE place with a structural pin that a second, unguarded entry point would fail. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8j Step-9 Finding, knowledge — orchestrator scoping miss in brief 206)*

**State:** DONE · `89eea8df` · 2026-07-28 (`admitPlanMutations` applied ONCE at `ingest-rewrite.ts:132-139`, placed so `touchedNotePaths` and `mergeStructural` both inherit it from the single `semantic` read — no second enforcement point; `deps.structural` deliberately merged in AFTER admission because its index.md/log.md targets ARE KN-12 parity) · **Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §6 (KN-10/KN-12), safety rule 1 · **Depends:** 13.8k · **Blocks:** the synthesis arming crossing

This is item 8(b)'s de-inlined-literal defect in the very entry warning about unread signals: prefer the command `rg -l '\bWithheldReason\b' --include='*.ts'` over the value**), and `planner.ts`'s `collectEntities` never reads `res.reason` at all. ⛔ **Do not read 13.8m-B landing as "refusals now reach the operator"** — that claim is SOURCE-path-only until 13.8m-C lands (the 13.8k module-header scoping precedent). · **Kind:** fix · **Spec:** §6 (KN-7 "rejected AND audited" — the governing requirement; the error-convention § is deliberately not cited, it sits outside Phase 13's anchor set) · **Depends:** 13.8k · **Blocks:** 13.8f-B's ability to distinguish refused-from-nothing-to-do

`admitGroundedPath` refuses correctly but the receipt has **no refusal channel**, so a poisoned-candidate attack produces a result byte-identical to a benign empty run — nothing is surfaced, nothing is audited, and 13.8f-B cannot tell "refused" from "nothing to do". That contradicts the repo's own KN-7 posture: rejected **and audited**, not merely rejected. Add a **code-only** `refusals: readonly GroundedPathRefusal[]` to the receipt (reason codes only — never paths or titles, rule 7) and have the worker consumer surface them. Deliberately NOT built in 13.8k because it is a receipt-shape change the worker consumes. **Done-when:** a refused path produces an observable, code-only refusal on the receipt; a benign empty run is distinguishable from a refused one; no path/title text crosses. *(origin: 2026-07-26 13.8k Step-9 Finding, knowledge)*

**Done-when:** `EntityRef`/`EntityKind` + a `.strict()` `EntityRefSchema` exist in `packages/contracts` per the house pattern, with the generated JSON Schema + field-set snapshot checked in and `registry-all.test.ts` green; the schema rejects a non-string/empty/whitespace `name`, an out-of-union `kind`, the three prototype-chain `kind` values (**L65**), unknown properties, and non-objects, while accepting all three valid kinds (non-vacuity); the Appendix-A + `packages/contracts/CLAUDE.md` mirror rows are written by the orchestrator; and leg 2's obligations (import re-point, local-declaration deletion, boundary gate call) are recorded. *(origin: ledger §DEC-CANDGATE, owner-approved 2026-07-26; numbered 2026-07-29 so it could be spec-linted)*

### 13.19 — §DEC-CANDGATE leg 2 (knowledge): call the schema at the `planSynthesis` boundary — the leg that actually closes the gate
- [~] PARTIAL · landed `93cafe5f` · 2026-07-29 — ⛔ **THE LEG THAT CHANGES BEHAVIOUR.** Leg 1 (13.18 `93ebeabd`) shipped the gate and deliberately closes NOTHING at runtime; this calls it. ⭐ **TICK CORRECTED 2026-07-30 — the box sat `[ ]` while the commit had landed**, found by the lead in a read-back rather than by a gate; worker read the stale record correctly and concluded leg 3 was undispatchable. **Five of six Done-when clauses VERIFIED against the commit** (import re-point · duplicate declaration deleted · `packages/evals` barrel untouched with its suite green · rejection surfaced-not-silently-dropped, counted · the 13.8h fan-out cap still applies, its ordering-vs-gate interaction deliberately pinned by composed tests). ⛔ **NOT MET — the immutability clause was ABSORBED, which is the failure mode the clause names verbatim.** `git show 93cafe5f -- packages/knowledge/src/synthesis/entity-resolver.ts` deletes knowledge's `readonly name` / `readonly kind` and re-exports contracts' `EntityRef`, whose fields are **not** readonly (`packages/contracts/src/models/entity-ref.ts:50-53`) ⇒ **type-level immutability was silently loosened at every knowledge consumer** — `planner.ts`, `meeting-rewrite.ts`, `attendee-refs.ts`, and via the barrel `packages/evals/src/synthesis/corpus.ts`, **another area's territory**. The commit message never mentions readonly/immutability, and 13.20 — the remedy this task pointed at (*"which can tighten it contracts-side"*) — shipped **tests-only**. ⚠ **SEVERITY, PRECISELY: a COMPILE-TIME guarantee was removed; the runtime candidate gate is UNAFFECTED and no mutation site is claimed** (not searched — establish, do not assume). ⇒ **remaining:** an OWNER/LEAD decision on the convention boundary — ⛔ **NOT the "tighten `EntityRef` readonly contracts-side" fix this line originally prescribed. That prescription was mine, it was written before it was scoped, and scoping INVERTED it.** ⭐ **Established 2026-07-30, all reproducible:** (i) **`readonly` is NOT the house convention in `packages/contracts/src/models/` — it is 0 of ~30 files.** No Appendix-A seam-model interface declares a `readonly` field: `task.ts:22-35`, `proposed-action.ts:23-31`, `project.ts:28-46`, `shared-shapes.ts:26-29` all plain, and `entity-ref.ts:25-27` self-documents that it *"matches `proposed-action.ts` / `task.ts`."* ⇒ **`EntityRef` lacking `readonly` is the NORM; adding it would make `EntityRef` the 1-of-30 OUTLIER.** (ii) **The real split is `readonly`-by-category, not by accident:** Zod-as-source seam models (`src/models/*.ts`) → never `readonly`; ad hoc TS-only shapes → often (`primitives/result.ts:4-10`, `api/ui-safe.ts`). `EntityRef` is squarely the first category. (iii) **`packages/knowledge` uses `readonly` pervasively** (`planner.ts` 34 readonly-lines/10 interfaces, `entity-resolver.ts` 13/3), which is exactly why the deleted knowledge-local `EntityRef` had it. ⇒ **THE DEFECT RESTATED CORRECTLY: leg 2 did not break a contracts convention — it silently swapped knowledge's local type (readonly, knowledge's convention) for contracts' (non-readonly, contracts' convention), CROSSING A CONVENTION BOUNDARY without reconciling it.** (iv) **ZERO mutation sites exist monorepo-wide** — scope: `grep -rn` for `.name =` / `.kind =`, `let`-declared `EntityRef` bindings, and `Object.assign` onto a ref, over all `*.ts`/`*.tsx` excluding `node_modules`; the only `.name =` hits are unrelated `Error` subclass constructors. Every consumer either constructs a fresh literal or reads fields. (v) **Adding `readonly` is compile-SAFE and a 1-file change** — verified empirically, not reasoned: a scratch compile at the repo's `strict` flags with `readonly` applied returns exit 0, a genuine mutation attempt is correctly rejected, and `EntityRefSchema`'s type params, `.strict()`, the generated JSON Schema, `__snapshots__/entity-ref.snap`, and `packages/evals` are all unaffected (JSON Schema has no concept of TS `readonly`). ⇒ ⛔ **SO THE QUESTION IS NOT "IS IT SAFE" (it is) BUT "IS IT RIGHT" — a documented house convention on a frozen-contract surface spanning three areas ⇒ escalation category 4, owner-decided via the lead.** Options as scoped: **(A)** add `readonly` to `EntityRef` only — restores the guarantee, creates a 1-of-30 inconsistency against a self-documented convention; **(B)** accept the loss — the guarantee was knowledge-local and knowledge-convention, contracts' convention is non-readonly, and nothing mutates anywhere; record the boundary; **(C)** re-establish immutability at knowledge's own consumption sites, honouring both conventions; **(D)** change the convention for all ~30 seam models — an arc, not a slice. ⚠ **No option is dispatchable until the owner rules; do NOT let a contract implementer pick one.** **leg 3 (worker) is NOT blocked by any of this** — the boundary gate call leg 3 would consume is landed and green. *(tick + finding 2026-07-30 orchestrator from the lead's read-back; remedy corrected the same day by a scoping pass that refuted the orchestrator's own prescription)*

## Phase 14 — Onboarding, Config Surfaces & Runtime Substrate
**Goal:** Give a real user a way to exist in the system, give the packaged app the local processes it needs to run, and stand up the two registries the spine resolves against (typed-Project registry; cross-workspace link store) — replacing `provisionDev` fixtures with real onboarding. Posture: pure-build / substrate — crosses no arming hard line (loopback-only; no external network / spend / credential). · **Spec anchors:** §19.1 (primary) · §11 · §13 · §5 · §8 · §6 · §4 · §16 · **Track:** desktop, worker · **Depends on (phases):** 3, 8, 9, 11
**Gate:** ✅ /phase-exit 14 CLEAR (2026-07-15) — checklist: docs/archive/IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md Part 3 · audits: docs/audits/14-reachability-desktop.md, 14-reachability-worker.md, 14-arch-drift.md

### 14.1 — Workspace onboarding & provisioning (real replacement for provisionDev)
- [x] DONE · `e1e4cfd` · 2026-07-15 · closes G58
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §19.1 · §11 · §5 (WS-8 registry) · **Depends:** P9 (§11 UI), P8 (§10 API), P3 (§5 WS-8 registry) · **Blocks:** 14.2, 14.5, 14.6, 14.7
**Files:** apps/desktop/renderer/surfaces/onboarding/, renderer/store/scope.ts, apps/worker/src/api/procedures/onboarding.ts, apps/worker/src/composition/provisionDev.ts (`provisionWorkspace`)
Real onboarding (create workspace → choose vault root → pick Simple/Professional/Founder/Advanced preset) mints workspaces and unions each id into the fail-closed WS-8 registry as the production provisioning path: `provisionWorkspace` builds a safe-default `Workspace` → upserts into the dual-dialect `WorkspaceConfigRepository` → unions into the WS-8 `{workspaceIds}` registry, **upsert-precedes-union** so a partial failure never yields a registry-known-but-config-less workspace. Isolation-class immutability guard: re-onboarding an existing id with a changed `type` → `workspace_type_immutable` (closes a rule-4/5 latent downgrade); same-type stays idempotent. vaultRoot maps to the existing `markdownRepoPath` — no frozen-`Workspace`-seam change; `preset` deferred to 14.5. Worker leg `e1e4cfd`; desktop onboarding UI + preset picker `ad624a16`. A workspace absent from the registry still gets zero read path (fail-closed).

### 14.2 — Connector configuration surface + per-workspace connector-instance registry
- [x] DONE · `6f639a6` · 2026-07-15 · closes G59
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §19.1 · §11 · §8 · **Depends:** 14.1, P6 (§8 gateways), P2 (§4 storage) · **Blocks:** Phase-16 composition, Phase-23 arming
**Files:** apps/desktop/renderer/surfaces/connectors/, packages/db connector-instance table + migration, apps/worker/src/api/procedures/connectorConfig.ts
A connectors/settings surface lets a user add a connector, bind it to a workspace, record its tokenRef, enable/pause it, and set cadence — each choice persisted as a per-vendor+workspace connector-instance record (the record Phase-16 composition + Phase-23 arming consume). tokenRef is **reference-only** (rule 7, structural whitelisting parser — no live call); register defaults **paused** (fail-safe); WS-8 membership-gated (registry-read-fault fails closed); `connector_instance_workspace_immutable` binding guard. NO real credential is provisioned here — binding a real credential/transport is Phase 17/23. Dual-dialect `connector_instance` table + `ConnectorInstanceRepository` db `bda2c91` + config path `6f639a6`; desktop connectors surface `7d141528`.

## Phase 18 — Real Model Transport & Intelligence Legs

HARD-LINE (crossing 2 — first real model transport + first real API spend); model-driven legs are eval-tested, deterministic gates stay TDD, every leg buildable dormant against the stub runner until the transport is bound. · **Spec anchors:** §19.5 (primary); §7 broker; §5 egress veto; §9 workflows 1&4; §6 KnowledgeWriter; §16 redaction; Appendix A (ProviderMatrix/ProviderRoute/AgentJob/ProposedAction/BrokerOutcome/Capability) · **Track:** providers-integrations · worker · **Depends on (phases):** 17 (Keychain `getSecret`), 15 (spine plumbing), 14 (app-managed Temporal + typed-Project registry), 16 (connector source content)

## Phase 19 — gbrain Write-Back, Parity, Provenance, Embedding-Egress & Cost Ledger

**Goal:** App owns vault→gbrain population and vault↔gbrain parity — post-commit incremental sync + durable outbox, HMAC provenance stamping on every KW commit, reconcile pass + real read/write gbrain clients, the rebuild-from-Markdown oracle, the serving-coverage 4-way gate, plus the two under-marked hard-lines (employer-embedding egress at the gbrain layer + a durable cross-run cost ledger upgrading the Phase-18 budget-gate port). Posture: HARD-LINE/mixed (crossing 3) — engines build dormant against fake clients; binding the real gbrain write/read clients + the real HMAC signing key + the employer-embedding backend is the crossing, owner confirm per crossing. · **Spec anchors:** §19.6 (primary); §6 write-through/divergence/parity + serving-coverage reader + reconcile trigger + provenance stamping; §4 operational stores (`GbrainSyncOutboxStore`, `ParityReportStore`, cost ledger); §5 embedding-egress veto; §7 budget gate; §16 redaction; Appendix A (ParityReport, SignedProvenanceStamp, GbrainReadGrant, FactProvenance). · **Track:** knowledge · worker · **Depends on (phases):** 18 (real KW-commit content + budget-gate port), 17 (Keychain HMAC signing key + `getSecret`), 14 (app-managed `gbrain serve` substrate)
**Gate:** — (no /phase-exit run)

### 19.10 — Employer-Work EMBEDDING egress at the gbrain layer

Before the 19.3 write client indexes any brain holding employer-work content, pin/verify gbrain's embedding backend is a LOCAL zero-egress model — the app's egress veto governs `ModelProviderPort` calls only, NOT gbrain's own embedding backend, so employer Markdown embedded by a cloud backend is a hidden egress crossing (safety rule 5 outside the veto). HARD LINE (crossing 3, owner confirm per crossing): indexing employer content FAILS CLOSED unless gbrain's embedding backend is a verified local zero-egress model; never a cloud-embedding fallback — OpenRouter / any cloud embedding is its own processor, not exempt (closes G65). **Done-when:** an employer-work brain whose embedding backend is cloud/unverified FAILS CLOSED at index time (no fact embedded); the same brain with a verified local zero-egress backend indexes normally; a leakage eval confirms zero raw employer content reaches a cloud embedding endpoint.

### Acceptance criteria (19)
- [ ] OPEN — all 11 task checkboxes unticked; no Phase-19 implementation commits (stub clients still default, real gbrain write/read clients + HMAC key + embedding backend unbound). With the real clients bound to a controlled local gbrain: a KW commit stamps an HMAC `SignedProvenanceStamp` (`verify` accepts; a tampered byte fails), enqueues a durable outbox row, and drives an idempotent index-apply that lands the fact exactly once — an apply failure never rolls back the commit (safety rule 3); the reconcile pass over the real read grant produces a clean-or-dirty `ParityReport` recorded record-only-on-ok feeding the coverage parity legs, a canonical-vs-DB divergence mints a `parity_defect` and degrades serving (safety rule 1); the rebuild-from-Markdown oracle sets `oracleBuildOk` and `deriveServingCoverage`'s 4-way AND-gate is green-CAPABLE, defeating any single leg degrades (go-live ADMISSION is Phase 20); an employer-work brain on a non-verified-local embedding backend FAILS CLOSED at index time, proven by a leakage eval showing zero raw employer content on any cloud embedding endpoint (safety rule 5); the durable cross-run cost ledger enforces COST-1/COST-2 across restarts via the 18.2 port (no forward/circular) and passes the one contract suite on both SQLite and Postgres. Every real-client leg is byte-equivalent to the shipped dormant default when the clients, HMAC key, and embedding backend are unbound (the owner-gated binds are the only behavior change); each `=== true` arming knob carries a truthy-not-`true` guard.

## Phase 22 — Propose Activation (last flip of the sequential chain)
**Goal:** Turn on the Copilot's only write path — `copilot.propose_action` (external) + `copilot.propose_knowledge` (semantic) — as the LAST flip of the sequential (1)–(6) chain behind a five-precondition gate, so every model-originated write lands a PENDING §9 Approval and nothing auto-applies. HARD-LINE crossing 6 — all §19.9 substrate is built + dormant; the flips are owner-gated (owner confirm per crossing) and every write stays human-gated per Approval even after arming; Phase 23 per-vendor enablement is NOT a precondition. · **Spec anchors:** §19.9 (activation home) · §8 · §6 · §5 · §9 · §3 (ProposedAction / KnowledgeMutationPlan / Approval frozen contracts) · **Track:** worker · **Depends on (phases):** 17, 18, 19, 20, 21
**Gate:** — (no /phase-exit run)

### 22.1 — Propose precondition-gate assertion (five preconditions across the sequential chain)
- [ ] OPEN
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §19.9 L580 (Propose Activation) + §5 propose gating / content-trust AND-term · **Depends:** Phase 17, Phase 18, Phase 19, Phase 20, Phase 21 · **Blocks:** 22.2, 22.3
**Files:** apps/worker/src/boot.ts:1628,1633 (extended — five-precondition guard ahead of `proposeEnabled` / `knowledgeProposeEnabled`)
Boot asserts the five arming preconditions before either propose flag is honored: (1) `deriveCopilotContentTrust` returns `'trusted'` (Phase 20), (2) `proofSpineParams !== undefined` — auto-ingest / KnowledgeWriter commit path provisioned (Phase 18), (3) the Keychain signing key resolves via `getSecret` (Phase 17), (4) the real external-write path is armed `WriteTransportGate.enabled === true` (Phase 21), (5) gbrain provenance stamping is real (Phase 19); any missing precondition holds propose OFF and mints an operator-visible `HealthItem` — never a flag-only override. Closes G52 (precondition / AND-term readiness half; the trust-flip itself is 20.4). Today boot has only the flag `===true` checks plus one `proofSpineParams` coupling — the unified composite gate is unbuilt. **Done-when:** with any one precondition absent, both propose paths resolve `read_only` and a System Health item names the missing precondition; boot surfaces `propose=OFF (reason=<precondition>)`.

## Phase 24 — OS One-Writer Enforcement, Hardening & Real Packaging

**Goal:** Make the one-writer guarantee OS-enforced (filesystem ACL + sole advisory-lock, not merely doctor-reported), guard every arming knob against truthy-coercion, round-trip retained-but-unread health items, ship a signed/notarized native-ABI DMG, and author the Part II arming runbook (safe turn-on order over the 12 phases / 7 crossings). Posture: pure-build — no hard-line crossing; safe to build to completion without an arming flip. · **Spec anchors:** §19.11 (activation home) · §6 (OS one-writer lockdown REQ-S-NEW-008) · §13 (packaging + arming runbook) · §4 (durable health-surface store) · §3 frozen HealthItem · **Track:** worker · knowledge · desktop · **Depends on (phases):** 14, 19, 17–23
**Gate:** — (no /phase-exit run)

### 24.1 — Upgrade REQ-S-NEW-008 doctor from report-only to active OS one-writer enforcement (ACL / advisory-lock)
- [ ] OPEN
**Kind:** build · **Spec:** §19.11 / §6 (REQ-S-NEW-008 OS one-writer lockdown: filesystem ACL + sole PGLite/vault advisory-lock) · **Depends:** Phase 19 · **Blocks:** 24.2
**Files:** apps/worker/test/install/doctor-posture.test.ts:1 · packages/knowledge/src/markdown-vault/atomic-write.ts:59
Upgrade the one-writer guarantee from a report-only doctor posture to an active OS-backed check so a stray write-capable gbrain process bound to a canonical brain is physically prevented from writing the canonical `.md`, not merely flagged; `atomic-write.ts` remains the in-process code-level floor and this is the OS floor beneath it (safety rule 1). This is the forward-home of the Phase-4 4.19 deferral (OS-level one-writer lockdown). At HEAD only the in-process floor exists — no advisory/flock/ACL lock acquisition present. **Done-when:** a second write-capable process attempting to bind the canonical brain is denied at the OS layer, verified by a test that spawns a contending writer and asserts the write is physically blocked, not just reported (closes G46).

### 24.6 — Safety-assertion audit: every claim DERIVED from the state that governs it, at the scope it applies to (⛔ RUN BEFORE GO-LIVE)
- [ ] OPEN — ⛔ **owner-approved 2026-07-26; required BEFORE go-live** (placed here, not on the Phase-9 exit — see the scoping note)
**Kind:** audit · **Spec:** §5 (egress/consent posture), §11 (surfaces), §16, safety rules 5/7 · **Depends:** — · **Blocks:** go-live
**Files:** audit output → `docs/audits/` · scope spans ALL areas + `docs/design/**` + `docs/**`
**Why:** this round produced FOUR rule-5 issues sharing one shape — a safety claim ASSUMED rather than derived (the hardcoded chrome "Egress: local-only" pill · the 9.10-A pre-store constant · the provisioning re-seed overwriting a revoked ack · the `zeroEgressOnly` contract drift) — plus a FIFTH with the inverse construction (the Copilot notice's absence implying safety). Each was found by building or reviewing, never by planning; contracts L56/L64 are the banked rules.
**⭐ HEADLINE FINDING THIS AUDIT EXISTS FOR — a TESTED false assurance (2026-07-26, worker 9.22).** An untested false assurance is a gap. A **tested** one is an ACTIVE DEFENSE of the defect: `egressCommands.test.ts:83` and `:178` both asserted `zeroEgressOnly === true` after a revoke, pinning a claim that was false whenever the workspace allowed a cloud processor. The suite therefore made the CORRECT fix look like a regression — the next person to attempt it sees red and backs out, **and backs out with the confidence a failing test confers.** That inverts the review gate from a check into a lock. ⚠ **Consequence for this audit's scope: an audit that reads only production code will not find this class at all — it lives in the ASSERTIONS.** Corollary observed in the same slice: after fixing a construction, re-sweep the TESTS for assertions encoding the old behaviour; worker reported one pinning assertion, the lead found a second (`:83`, a full-object `toEqual` — the more brittle form), and an orchestrator sweep found two further stale FAKES modelling a now-impossible state. One report, four artifacts (contracts L61/L69).

**⛔ FOUR BINDING CONSTRAINTS (owner-set; do not soften any of them):**
1. **CONCEPT-LEVEL enumeration, never known string forms.** The orchestrator's own design-doc sweep needed THREE iterations because the first grepped the phrasings already known — 4 of 9 sites found. **A string-shaped audit returns a confident false all-clear, which is worse than not running it** (contracts L64). Report the search KEY and the dry-iteration count.
2. **BOTH mechanisms.** Asserted-constant (a claim stated from a constant/default-seed) AND **fail-open-by-omission** (a claim implied by a MISSING element — e.g. a warning that renders only when data is present, so "no data" and "nothing to warn about" look identical). They look nothing alike in code and a sweep tuned to one will miss the other.
3. **DOCS AND DESIGN AUTHORITY IN SCOPE, not just code.** The chrome pill's mandate lived in a locked screen-GENERATION prompt and in a normative governance principle — **a code-only audit would have cleared it** while the generator kept reproducing the defect.
4. **The question is "is this claim DERIVED from the state that governs it, at the SCOPE that state applies to" — NOT "is this claim currently true."**
5. **TEST ASSERTIONS AND FAKES ARE IN SCOPE** (added 2026-07-26 from the headline finding above). A safety claim can be defended by a test that pins it, or normalized by a fake that models it — neither appears in production code. Sweep assertions and fixtures with the same concept-level method, and treat a test asserting a safety value as a claim requiring the same derivation check as the code. A claim that happens to be true today but cannot move when the governing state moves is a decoration that reads as a guarantee.
**Scoping note (orchestrator's call, 2026-07-26):** deliberately NOT ridden on the Phase-9 exit. Its scope is wider than any single phase (both mechanisms · all areas · docs + design authority, which is not Phase-9 territory), and the Phase-9 gate is already blocked on 9.21 plus five `[~]` legs — hanging a cross-cutting pre-go-live audit off it would either delay the gate or get the audit rushed to unblock it. Phase 24 is where hardening lives and is pre-go-live by construction.
**Done-when:** an audit report in `docs/audits/` covering both mechanisms across code + docs + design authority, stating the concept-level search keys used and the number of iterations to dry, with every finding either fixed or recorded as a task; a one-pass string-shaped sweep does NOT satisfy this. *(origin: 2026-07-26 owner approval via lead, after the round's fifth same-shape rule-5 issue)*

### Acceptance criteria (24)
- [ ] OPEN
All 24.X ticked; the one-writer guarantee is OS-enforced (ACL/advisory-lock) with a contending write-capable process physically blocked, proven by a spawn-a-contender test not a report; every arming knob resolves OFF for all truthy-but-not-`true` inputs (incl. string `'false'`), pinned by regression guard-tests, dead boot fields pruned; retained-but-unread `HealthItem`s round-trip to the renderer and persist read-state across restart; the Part II arming runbook documents the full 12-phase / 7-crossing turn-on order (sequential chain + independent per-vendor arc), each crossing's gate/flags/smoke, with each hard-line phase's Done-when citing its runbook step (closes G57). Phase smoke: a stray write-capable gbrain process bound to a canonical brain is physically blocked; every knob set to `'false'` resolves OFF; a `HealthItem` minted while the panel was closed appears unread on open; a signed+notarized DMG installs and launches with the native-ABI worker and its bundled runtimes; the runbook's turn-on order is internally consistent with every hard-line phase's Done-when.

## Decisions tabled

- **Schema-snapshot coverage scope** — RESOLVED at the contract layer 2026-06-30 (all 27 Appendix-A seam models covered); the residual decision survives only for downstream-phase artifacts (archive Part 1).
- **Config-default deferrals** (Phase-0 config, not contract changes): LIFE-2 catch-up window · LIFE-1 lease TTL/heartbeat + fencing-token location · crash-loop threshold · backup cadence · default `maxCostUsd` · per-preset composition. Built configurable with documented defaults; confirm before V1 ship.
- **GBrain live 4-condition round-trip validation → Phase 12 (12.7/12.22/12.23)** — the enablement gate for the per-workspace `writeThroughEnabled` flip (decision itself resolved, ADR-007; only the live proof is deferred). Related: the **gbrain pin-identity sub-decision** (SHA vs semver — gbrain exposes no local commit-SHA; task 11.3 + §ARM-GBRAIN step 3).
- **Local-provider conformance (Ollama / LM Studio)** — operator-deferred; both stay `conformanceStatus: unknown` (matrix-ineligible). Architecturally required as the §5 Employer-Work zero-egress route — validate before any local egress path ships.
- **Native-key provider conformance (Anthropic/OpenAI direct)** — extraction now runs Option-B subscription (§ARM-18), so native-key conformance stays deferred; OpenRouter routes live-verified. Per-token pricing for OpenAI/OpenRouter: `costEstimation.pricing` null-with-cited-source; operator fills.
- **Signed + notarized DMG (hardened runtime)** — V1.1 (§15); unsigned build-from-source proven for V1.
- **§DEC-CAT4 — FailureClass security members** (see Owner-Gates; frozen-contract, owner's call).
- **Workspace reclassification path (owner-gated — recorded 2026-07-15, lead ruling).** Workspace `type`/isolation-class and every workspace-binding anchor (`workspace_type_immutable`, `project_workspace_immutable`, `connector_instance_workspace_immutable` — 14.1/14.6/14.2, worker L30) are **immutable-by-default as the safe floor**. The ONLY escape hatch is a deliberate, owner-gated, audited **reclassification** operation that re-scopes/re-indexes the resource (rule 4/5, §5/§19.1) — a future session must NEVER satisfy a rebind need by weakening the immutability guard in place.
- **Accepted redaction residual (§16 boundary).** A secret mislabeled under a system id field (`correlationId`/`*Id`/`*Ref`) passes the id-charset gate — an accepted, recorded boundary of the redaction design (phase-10 exit), not an open defect.
- **FailureVariant→FailureClass collapse (recorded).** `validation_rejected`+`schema_rejected` collapse to `schema_rejection`; `degraded_unavailable`→`worker_down`. Deliberate taxonomy mapping — cross-linked to §DEC-CAT4 + Carry-forward #5 (open enum members).
